**Z-9 went ANSWERED → OPEN.** Its cell reads `OPEN **ANSWERED AT THE MECHANISM LEVEL 2026-09-03**…`
— the author marked it open and then described a partial advance in bold. **The old scanner skipped
the leading `OPEN` and counted the row as fully answered.**

> **A row that begins with the word OPEN was being counted as closed**, and it was counted that way
> because a *different* row's typography broke the parser's pairing.

**Both ambiguous rows were rewritten rather than the classifier tuned to guess them.** Z-9 and BQ-5
now lead with `**PARTLY ANSWERED …**`, which is what their authors described. **Tuning a reader to
recover an ambiguous row teaches it to guess; fixing the row teaches nothing and stays fixed.**

**Net: ANSWERED 148 → 148, PARTIAL 29 → 31, OPEN 422 → 420.** The headline number did not move, and
**that is the point** — one row was being over-counted and one under-counted, and a checker whose
totals look stable can still be wrong about every row underneath them.

---

## The question ledger is consistent, and getting there broke three more readers (2026-09-03)

**`questions consistent: the table and the document checklists agree`** — first clean run.

**1. An unknown status word now says so instead of defaulting to OPEN.** `repos-78` measured the
silent fall-through; with the lead-cell fix in place **21 rows across 21 distinct lead words** had a
status the vocabulary did not know — `DISSOLVED`, `DONE`, `NEGATIVE NOW EXHAUSTIVE`,
`MEASURED PROPERLY: 21, not 53`, `HALF ANSWERED`, `NOT A DEFECT`.

> **An unknown status word was indistinguishable from an unanswered question.** That silence was the
> defect, not the vocabulary size. The reader now returns `UNKNOWN`; **the 21 rows were normalised
> rather than 21 keywords added**, because teaching a reader synonyms teaches it to guess.

**A new category was needed, and that is different from a synonym.** `WITHDRAWN` covers *dissolved*,
*superseded* and *not a defect* — **a question whose premise was wrong is neither answered nor
outstanding.** Counting it OPEN claims work that does not exist; counting it ANSWERED claims a
finding nobody made. **5 rows.** Result: ANSWERED 149 → 158, PARTIAL 31 → 38, WITHDRAWN 5.

**2. A change I measured and did NOT adopt.** `progress.py` verdicts *every* cell of a row while
`check_questions.py` reads the answer cell, and the two disagreed by 29. The obvious fix — make
progress read the last cell too — **would have broken 14 rows**, because the `PROPOSED-*` block has
a different column count and the last cell there is not the status.

> **The register is not one table. It is several table shapes in one file, and any single-rule
> parser over it misreads some rows.** `progress.py`'s scan-every-cell is an accommodation for that,
> not a bug — **I nearly "fixed" it into being worse, and the only thing that stopped me was
> measuring the change before adopting it.**

**3. A cross-reference was being read as a claim.** A box asking `K-8` ends
`See … OPEN-QUESTIONS.md **Z-10**.` and was reported as an unticked box for the ANSWERED question
`Z-10`. Worse, the id pattern required `.` or `*` right after the id, so `**K-8 (new).**` **never
matched its own id** — the box was attributed to the reference *and to nothing else*.

**Fixing it took the stale count from 1 to 7, and all seven were real.** *The number going up was
the fix working.*

**4. Sub-questions collapsed onto their parents.** `B-2/a`–`B-2/e` all matched `B-2`, so **five open
sub-parts read as five stale boxes for an answered parent.** The parent *is* answered; the sub-parts
are new questions it spawned. **The distinction matters exactly where `repos-78`'s Z-9 lesson does:
a ticked box with an unread half is how a question stops being asked** — but an answered question
that spawned five new ones is not that, and conflating them would have downgraded a correct answer.
Five rows added; the ledger went clean.

---

## 0 callees reported, 15 present — and a 2,440-byte body reported as zero (2026-09-03)

`002AAE40` decompiles in full and contains **fifteen distinct direct calls** — `0039C4A0`,
`0017F880`, `002B2150`, `002B1F20`, `002AC5F0`, `002AC2F0`, `0020E600`, `0017F8C0`, `0017FC90`,
`0017F850`, `0017FA10`, `0017FD30`, `0017FB00`, `0017F7C0`, `002B4730`, `00808C40`.

| query | answer |
|---|---|
| `get_function_callees(002AAE40)` | **`[]`** |
| `get_function_by_address(002AAE40)` | `body_start == body_end == 002AAE40` — **zero bytes** |
| the exported function list | **2,440 bytes** |
| `decompile_function` | **the whole body** |
| `disassemble_bytes(002AB5B0)` | `call 0x002AC5F0` — a direct call with a literal target |

**The calls are not indirect and the code is not missing.** The live program simply has no function
body defined over the extent, and every query keyed on that body returns nothing **without saying
that nothing is what it has.**

> **CLAUDE.md already says a negative from a call-graph query is not proof of absence. This is that
> rule with a number on it: 0 reported, 15 present.**

### The doubt this raised was checked and withdrawn

It looked as though the residual survey's *"purest example … no caller"* might be describing the
empty body rather than the code. **It is not.** The survey used the snapshot call graph, and
`Tools/work/graph/edges.tsv` records `002AAE40` with **16 outgoing edges and 0 incoming**, against
`002AC5F0`'s 44 and 7. **The absent callers are real; only the live program's view is empty.**

> **An unfounded doubt hung on someone else's correct finding is its own defect** — it reads as a
> caveat, survives in the text, and costs every later reader the same check. Withdrawn in both
> places it was written.

### What survives, and it is operational

Sampling 15 residuals, **8 have `body_start == body_end` in the live program** — `002AAE40`,
`004EDB50`, `004F5360`, `002B8EE0`, `002F0280`, `0020AC30`, `001D72F0`, `001C2980` — while
`edges.tsv` carries their edges normally (`002B8EE0` 19 out, `001D72F0` 17 out, `001C2980` 13 out).

> **Use `edges.tsv` for any call-graph question about a residual.** `get_function_callees` answers
> `[]` for roughly half of them and does not say that `[]` is what it has.

**Two sources, one complete and one not, and the incomplete one is the one with the friendlier
interface.** That is the whole hazard: the MCP query is a single call and the TSV needs an `awk`, so
the wrong source is the one that gets reached for.

---

## A sixth scored dimension, and where its denominator came from (2026-09-03)

`registry_explained` — **5 of 63 authored-data registry slots**, weight 0.05.

**The denominator is the point.** A "formats covered" metric with an authored denominator grades
itself: plan seven sheets, write four, report 57 %. Pick a smaller plan, score higher. So the
population is not a plan — it is the **authored-data pointer registry** established at tier 1 in
[authored-data-table-registry](../02-Resource-And-Asset-Pipeline/authored-data-table-registry.md):
bounds `0x02EBF008`–`0x02EBF1F8`, 8-byte stride, **63 slots**, scanned from `.text`, reopened three
times, with one of its own claims already overturned.

**`check_formats.py` re-derives that count rather than trusting it** — `(end − start) / stride + 1`
— and **refuses to score if the arithmetic disagrees with the number the document states.** The
self-test breaks the document on purpose to prove the guard fires.

**The numerator is declared, not scraped.** A document claims a slot in frontmatter:

```yaml
registry_slots:
  - "0x02EBF0C0"
```

Prose-scanning would have been wrong by a mile: `0x02EBF138` is *mentioned* in ten documents and
`0x02EBF190` in nine. **A mention is not an explanation**, and a declared claim has an owner, so a
slot claimed twice is reported as a collision under the standing no-address-described-two-ways rule.

**One assignment was stopped by the corpus itself.** `0x02EBF138` looked like it belonged to the
`st2e` document, which shows `action_data.bin` with 543 records against the registry's 543 ids —
**but that document has already downgraded the binding to a lead and carries AY-6 to prove it.**
The slot stayed with the registry. *A count match is suggestive, not deciding* — its own falsification
pass said so, and it was right to.

**Container formats are counted and not scored.** `save-image`, `st2e`, `vbf-bigfile-index`,
`game-settings-ini` — four, reported as a bare number. **There is no censused universe of container
formats, so any percentage would be invented.**

> **A number without a denominator is honest; a number with a made-up one is not.**

### The re-weighting, and why the headline fell 4.58 points

The 0.05 came out of `ledger_resolved` (0.20 → 0.15). **Overall 44.45 % → 39.87 %, with not one
line of the database changed.**

| shave 0.05 from | overall | change |
|---|---:|---:|
| `ledger_resolved` (at 99.55 %) | **39.87 %** | **−4.58** |
| `engine_explained` (at 19.87 %) | 43.85 % | −0.60 |

> **The headline can be moved several points by choosing which dimension to shave.** That alone is
> the reason the choice has to be made about the deliverable and never about the resulting number.

`ledger_resolved` is **saturated** — 99.55 %, moving 0.003 points per residual — so it was
contributing a near-constant 0.199 for work that is finished. **The drop is the finding:** the
headline had been partly propped up by a completed dimension. Taking the weight from the largest pot
instead would have cost a tenth as much and taught nothing.

`progress.py --show-reweight` prints both figures side by side, so the step is visibly a
re-weighting and not a regression.

---

## The census blindness that only a file census could find — 2026-09-03

Seven blindness modes are catalogued above, and all seven are ways a **binary** census miscounts.
There was an eighth, of a different kind, and no instrument in the kit could see it.

**The database reads engines well and the files they eat barely at all.**

| subsystem | documents | owned functions | its own files |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Audio | 11 | 2,064 | `.sab` 4,609 + `.snd` 2,304 — **one passing mention between them** |

Nothing was wrong with the audio documents. The gap was invisible because **`engine_explained`
counts functions cited, and a format nobody documents costs no functions.** A database could reach
100 % of the binary and still not describe a single shipped file.

> **A metric set is blind to whatever it does not count, and that blindness is silent.** Every check
> in the kit was passing. The hole was found by censusing the archive — an axis nothing measured —
> not by any check reporting anything.

### Why `formats_explained` may be scored when `container_format` may not

`container_format:` was deliberately counted and not scored, for a stated reason: *"no censused
universe of container formats exists, so a percentage would be invented."* **That was correct, and
it has now been falsified for one half of the population and not the other.**

`FFXII_TZA.vbf` indexes every file the game ships. The population of archive formats is therefore a
`GROUP BY` over **89,414 entries → 48 extensions**, generated by `census_archive_formats.py`. It is
not a matter of opinion and not something an author can shrink.

Formats that ship **loose** — the save image, the VBF container itself, `GameSetting.ini`, the
`.fst` tables — still have no census. **They keep `container_format:` and stay unscored.** The rule
did not change; the facts changed under one half of it.

### The denominator keeps the boring rows on purpose

`.ico` (2 files), `.tga` (2) and `.h` (6) sit in the same denominator as `.phyre` (35,984). Trimming
a census to the interesting rows is an author choosing what they will be graded against — the exact
defect `check_ebp.py` exists to prevent. They are cleared by **disposition**, not by exclusion.

**And the disposition is where the gaming risk moved to**, so the bar is mechanical:
`standard` and `external` are **refused without a note naming the standard or the reference**, and a
refused entry does not count. The cheap disposition is the one that forces a checkable claim.

### Third shave off the same dimension, and the last one

`formats_explained` took its 0.05 from `ledger_resolved`, which has now fallen `0.20 → 0.15 → 0.10
→ 0.05` in one day. The saturation argument still holds — 99.55 %, near-constant, carrying no
information — **but it holds three times and would hold forever, which is the problem.**

> **At 0.05 the dimension is at its floor and the next new dimension must be funded elsewhere.**
> Shaving the same saturated pot a fourth time would stop being a judgement about information
> content and become a habit that never costs anything. And it must not be deleted either: a
> resolved ledger is the precondition for every other figure here, and a metric that stops watching
> a finished thing is how it quietly comes undone.

### A known weakness in the new denominator, recorded before anyone finds it

**The census counts extensions, not formats.** `.dat` has 2,948 entries across two different trees
(`ps2data/image` 2,885, `gamedata/d3d11` 63) — exactly the shape of several unrelated formats
sharing a name. If that is what it proves to be, this denominator is **wrong in the direction that
flatters it**, and that is a finding to write up, not a number to quietly change.

### The extension-vs-format weakness, measured and closed — 2026-09-03

The section above recorded a self-criticism: *"the census counts extensions, not formats… if `.dat`
turns out to be several formats sharing an extension, this denominator is wrong in the direction
that flatters it."* **It was measured the same day, and the answer was more interesting than the
worry.**

`census_format_magics.py` samples archive entries through the SRYK index, decompressing only each
entry's first block, and groups them by leading signature. **32 of 48 extensions show exactly one
signature.** The error runs **both ways**:

| direction | case | effect on the denominator |
|---|---|---|
| several extensions, one format | **`PKFL` = `.mot` `.snd` `.vpc` `.vpcjp`** (10,314 files), `@FRP` = `.ca` `.cm`, `TIM2` = `.tm2` `.nfs`, `/Gam` = `.ah` `.ta` | **over-counts** formats |
| one extension, several formats | **`.bin` — 7 signatures in 12 samples**, including the already-documented `st2e` | **under-counts** |

> **The denominator was NOT re-cut.** A generated census an author reshapes after seeing the result
> stops being a census — that is the whole reason it is generated. The mapping is published instead,
> `check_assets.py` enforces the consequence (a sheet explaining a layout must declare every
> extension in its signature group), and the residual distortion is stated in the open.

**`.dat`, the row the worry named, was the tame one** — `himg` in 10 of 12 samples. **The instrument
found the problem somewhere else than the place suspicion pointed**, which is the ordinary result of
measuring instead of arguing.

**Stated limit:** twelve samples per extension. A format present in 1 % of an extension's files will
usually be missed, so a single signature is **evidence of homogeneity, never proof**. And a split is
equally the shape of a version field in the leading dword — neither script calls that either way.

### A name is a weak instrument: four guesses falsified in one pass

`ext_string_probe.py` asks whether the binary names an extension and prints the surrounding run.
**42 of 47 are named**, many as full path templates — and four claims written the same day died:

| claim | verdict |
|---|---|
| *"`dbg` and `lst` are build artefacts by name"* | **False.** `.lst` is `qstl`; the binary calls it `quest\menuquest.lst`. |
| *"`.ico` is probably a PS2 icon, not a Windows one"* | **False.** The magic is exactly `00 00 01 00`. |
| *"`ah` `ta` `ker` `fex` are Phyre neighbours"* | **False.** Text asset lists and font-system files; sharing `gamedata/d3d11` meant nothing. |
| *"`.mot` is a motion format"* | **Probably false.** It is `PKFL`, shared with three audio extensions — a package holding motion, not a motion format. |

**Every one of the four was name-driven or location-driven**, and both instruments that overturned
them read bytes. That is the standing lesson, now with four fresh instances.

> **The probe's own noise floor is printed rather than assumed.** `.h` reports 36 hits, of which 17
> are in `.text` — code bytes like `D$.H`, not strings. And **absence still proves nothing**:
> `.efb`, `.sav`, `.dbg`, `.msb`, `.sbn` produce no string, exactly as `PClusterHeaderBase` produces
> none while carrying eighteen recovered fields.

### Two shipped corpora name the engine's own internals — 2026-09-03

Found while auditing what the archive contains, not by any check reporting anything. **The game
ships the engine's parameter vocabulary in plain text**, and nothing in the database used it.

`jsondata/maps/*.json` — 459 files, one per area, **175 distinct keys**: `mDofFocusDistance`,
`mSAOBiasSce`, `mShadowValueLerpMinForMap`, `m_iPCFBlurSizeForMap`. `jsondata/cutscenes` adds 337
more keyed `Cut 1`…`Cut N`. `prefetchdata` adds ~1,040 files per language across nine languages,
each a scene's asset list under a declared `##Total_data_Size:_N_MB` budget.

**These are oracles, and an oracle is never an authority** — the same standing `ff12-script` has for
EBP. But the join promotes them: `census_jsondata_names.py` asks whether the shipping binary carries
each key as a literal, and **137 of 175 are present**, each one xref from its reader. Present is
tier 2; absent stays tier 9 and may not name anything.

> **A plain-text name is unusually dangerous evidence precisely because it reads like source.** The
> tier-9 cap exists for exactly this shape of temptation, and the join is what lifts it honestly.

### The absent half was the better finding, and two signals agreed

Of the 38 keys with no literal, **27 carry authoring-tool markers** — `mCascadeNumberCheckBox-
CheckState`, `mCascadePartition0LockCheckBoxCheckState`, `…UseStateCheckBoxCheckState`.
**Square Enix's internal lighting-editor UI state ships inside the retail game data.**

**No tool-marked key is in the binary, and none of the 137 present keys is tool-marked.** Two
independent classifications, zero contradictions. **Agreement between two signals is evidence; one
signal renamed twice is not**, which is why the classification is reported beside the string result
and never merged into it.

### An absence claim with a proper positive control

```
mMapAmbientColorR   70 files   008FA750
mMapAmbientColorG   52 files   008FA768     <- three siblings, consecutive, all found
mMapAmbientColorB   46 files   008FA780
mMapAmbientColorA   18 files   -- no literal --
```

**The instrument demonstrably finds this family and does not find the alpha.** That satisfies the
standard's positive-control requirement, and it yields a real modder fact: the ambient alpha is
authored in eighteen files and read by nothing.

**Eleven keys remain genuinely open** — absent and not tool-marked. `mCascadePartition0_2` and `0_3`
appear in **244 files each**, and a numbered family that widespread is far likelier read through a
constructed name or an array index than not read at all. **A constructed name leaves no literal**,
which is the standing reason absence is never proof of inertness.

### Why this is not a ninth dimension

It names things; it does not add a population to explain. The keys feed `engine_explained` and
17-Presentation directly, and the files are already counted once under `formats_explained`.
**Scoring them again would count the same work twice** — the defect §3 of the completion plan
exists to prevent.

### The staleness detector was itself stale — 2026-09-03

Sealing checkpoint **GN160** (batch `R55TEXT`, 15 residuals classified, unresolved 154 → 139)
exposed a defect in the tool whose entire job is finding stale stamps.

`check_provenance.py` kept its **own hardcoded copy** of the certified parent sha. When GN160 was
sealed, the checker was found naming **GN158 — two generations behind** — and printing
`<-- current parent` beside a superseded checkpoint. It had already been wrong through the whole of
GN159 and nothing complained.

> **A hardcoded constant cannot disagree with itself.** The checker compared 166 documents against
> a value no other part of the system had confirmed, and reported "consistent" for the subset that
> matched an obsolete parent. **Every check it ran was a check against the wrong question.**

The fix is not a new constant. `$GfzParentSha` in `GfzEnv.ps1` is the single source of truth — its
own header says *"UPDATE THIS BLOCK after every certified checkpoint, or every later run works from
a stale parent and silently re-does closed work"* — so the checker now **reads** it and **names the
source it used**. The fallback constant survives only for a missing environment file, and it says
so out loud; an unlabelled fallback is how the same defect returns.

**This is the `hand-maintained indexes drift; check them mechanically` rule catching a second
instance, in the mechanism built to enforce the first.**

### Sealing a checkpoint converts closed work into provenance debt

`STALE OR MISSING PARENT STAMP` moved **66 → 166**: every document in the database is now stamped
to a parent that has been superseded.

**That is arithmetic, not a regression.** The rule stands — *do not rewrite the stamps; re-verify
and then update them* — so the debt is paid by re-verification, one document at a time, and the
number is supposed to look bad until it is.

Worth stating plainly because the temptation runs the other way: **the cheapest way to make this
number zero is a search-and-replace across 166 files**, which would assert 166 verifications that
never happened. The checker's own closing line already forbids it.

---

## The EBP round, six sessions in parallel — 2026-09-03

**Merged by `repos-55` from five inbox reports.** Per-section corpus statistics live in the sheets
that own them; what follows is only what the *instruments* did.

### A census that was accurate, insufficient, and nearly published a false `Inert`

**`repos-4b` ran a whole-program instruction-level reference census** on the `CML1` blob pointer
`0x022C6D80` — `DumpDataXrefs.java`, Ghidra's `ReferenceIterator`, all reference types. It returned
**four references**: a store in the load callback, a store in module init, and a load-plus-store in
teardown where the loaded value's only use is `free`.

**On that evidence `section_008` was entirely inert — a whole section a modder could edit with no
effect.** A striking finding, and wrong.

`0x0032FA43` calls `0x0026CE00` **with the blob still live in RDX** (`mov edx, ecx` / `and rdx, rax`
at `0x0032FA25`/`0x0032FA36`, never clobbered), and that callee copies the pointer into the
actor-manager record at `+0xD8`. Consumers reach it from there. The real reader,
`CML1_ResolveNames` at `0x003DA1F0`, dereferences `blob[+0x04]` at `0x003DA253`.

> **The search stopped counting exactly where the copy started.** This is the displacement-census
> blind spot already catalogued above, in a new disguise: not a base pointer this time but a *live
> register crossing a call boundary*. **A reference census counts references; it cannot count
> aliases.**

**What caught it was reading the call site rather than trusting the census** — prompted by
`repos-1e` independently calling `0x0026CE00` a binder.

**And the repair changed the shape of the claim, which is the durable lesson.** The `Inert` rows
that survive in `section-008.md` §3 are stated **against the reader's own 205-byte body** — *"this
field is written before anything reads it"* — not against a search having been exhaustive. **That
is a materially stronger claim and it does not depend on any census being complete.** Prefer it
wherever an `Inert` row is asserted.

`repos-c7` reached the same discipline from the other direction: it attacked its own *"nothing reads
`CML1`"*, found the chunk pointer survives in `rdx` and that `FUN_0026d3a0` stores a second copy,
and therefore wrote **"no reader found by X"** rather than "inert". Two sessions, one conclusion:
**an absence claim about a pointer must survive the aliasing question or be phrased as a failed
search.**

### The scored EBP denominator now has three independent derivations

| route | by | result |
|---|---|---|
| external unpacker census | earlier work | the frozen 13-section population |
| the container's **own directory**, all 1,114 files | `census_ebp_container.py` | all 13 agree exactly |
| re-derived per section during authoring | `repos-3f` (828 / 359), `repos-4b` (423 / 423) | agree exactly |

**A denominator three separate routes reproduce is worth more than one nobody has re-checked** —
and this one is scored, which is precisely where that matters.

### The constant-scan negative held, and a better instrument replaced it

The marker scan was retired here for a measured reason: **noise floor 84**, which swamps six of
thirteen real section indices. `repos-1e` did not repeat it. It read **the dispatch table out of one
function's branch structure instead — all 14 slots, zero false positives.**

**The comparison is the useful part.** The branch reading found `section_017`, which has a live
handler and occurs in **0 of 1,115** files — an index the scan could never have separated from
noise. And it showed the six no-handler slots that the scan had reported as *present*, `001`
scoring 518 against a floor of 84.

> **A negative result is only half the work. The other half is naming the instrument that does
> work**, and here it was branch structure rather than constant frequency.

### Two discipline notes worth keeping

**`repos-3f` re-read an entire loader contract from raw instruction bytes via capstone**, against
the authorized EXE and independent of Ghidra's decompiler. Both agree. That is the decompiler-
invention attack in `FORMAT.md` §6 actually executed rather than ticked.

**`repos-3f` watched a scored dimension move underneath it mid-block** — `ebp_explained` read 6/14
on its post-write run and 8/14 minutes later as other lanes landed — and **reported it as observed
rather than as a claim about the total.** That is the concurrency rule working: with six sessions
writing, a metric read is a snapshot of a moving target, and only the coordinator's read is a
statement about the whole.

### A coordinator-authored instruction that was wrong

**`ASSIGNMENTS.md` §2 gave a relative `-ProjectDir`, which cannot work.** Every session had to
discover that independently and absolute paths are required. Recorded because the board is an
instrument too: **a briefing defect costs once per reader, and this one cost five times.**

---

## `0x`-prefixed addresses were invisible to every citation gate — 2026-09-04

**Found by `repos-c9` while working the audit-debt lane**, which is worth stating first: the
assignment was to attack sheets it had not written, and what it found was a defect in the
instruments — one that made **its own sheets invisible**.

### Two variants of one blindness, across nine scripts

**`\b` does not fire after `0x`.** In `0x003304A0` the character before the leading `0` is `x`, a
word character, so there is no word boundary and `\b(0[0-9A-Fa-f]{7})\b` never matches. The gate
saw bare `003304A0` and was blind to the prefixed form.

**Backtick-anchored patterns failed for a different reason.** `` `(0[0-9A-Fa-f]{7})` `` requires the
backtick immediately before the `0`, so `` `0x0023A570` `` slipped through while `` `0023A570` ``
matched.

`repos-c9` measured it across 227 documents containing addresses:

| | |
|---|---:|
| bare-form addresses the gates could see | **31,001** |
| **`0x`-prefixed, silently dropped** | **2,856** |
| **documents 100 % invisible to the citation family** | **17** |
| documents mixing both forms, losing the `0x` half | 137 |

**Eight of the seventeen were EBP sheets** written in this round — by `repos-c9` itself, `repos-4b`
and `repos-c7`. **The gates reported those documents as citing nothing at all**, which is
indistinguishable from a document that genuinely cites nothing.

### `progress.py` had it too, and nobody had checked

The scored dimension used the same backtick form. Measured **before** fixing, so the correction is
auditable rather than asserted:

| | cited functions |
|---|---:|
| old pattern | 6,851 |
| `0x`-aware | **6,923** |
| **silently dropped** | **72** — worth **+0.12 points** at weight 0.55 |

**Small in the headline and not small in kind.** It is an UNDER-count in the deliverable dimension,
and it grows with every sheet written in the `0x` style — which is what the current round is
producing. A defect that scales with output is worse than its size today.

> **The fix already existed in the codebase.** `check_located_not_read.py` line 123 used
> `(?<![0-9A-Fa-f])...(?![0-9A-Fa-f])` — lookarounds rather than `\b` — and was therefore correct
> all along. **Eight scripts had the bug and one had the cure, and nothing compared them.** That is
> the hand-maintained-index failure in a new place: nine copies of one pattern, drifting
> independently.

Repaired in `progress.py`, `verify_doc_addresses.py`, `check_self_contradiction.py`,
`check_located_not_read.py`, `gen_address_index.py` and `audit_inbox_backlog.py`. `check_formats.py`
is untouched and correct — it *deliberately* requires `0x` for registry slots.

**Immediate consequence:** the repaired gate now reports **29 documents with `.text` non-function
citations to confirm** — real audit work that was hidden while the pattern could not see it.

### The general lesson, stated because it will happen again

**A citation gate that cannot see a legal way of writing an address does not report an error — it
reports success.** Every one of those 17 documents passed `check_named_not_cited.py` cleanly by
appearing to cite nothing. **Silence from an instrument is not evidence, and this is the third time
this session that has been the finding** (the others: a linear `.text` sweep returning 0 of 63
registry slots, and a PKFL census announcing "0 never present" over a range that could not contain
an empty slot).

### The same regex ALSO over-counts — and this was reported once already and lost

**`repos-d6` checked its own sheet against `repos-c9`'s finding and found the opposite error in the
same pattern.** The two compose badly.

Measured on `section-016.md`:

| | |
|---|---:|
| distinct addresses the document actually cites | **40** |
| what the gate saw | 12 |
| of those, real citations | 11 |
| of those, **not an address at all** | **1** |
| **real citations actually checked** | **11 of 40 — 27.5 %, and the gate reported `ok`** |

The spurious one is `01206005`, matched inside the decimal literal **`1.01206005`**. `0x01206005`
lands inside a real PE section, so the gate **resolves it, classifies it as a `data` citation, and
passes.** It does not fail — **it silently counts a float as evidence.**

> **A document can present fewer citations than it has AND have the remainder contaminated by things
> that are not addresses, with `ok` printed over both.**

**The coordinator's own fix did not cover this.** The `0x` repair used
`(?<![0-9A-Fa-fx])`, which still admits a match after a decimal point — so `0.00390625` and
`1.01206005` continued to match. **Measured across the database: 100 spurious decimal-fragment
matches in 22 documents.** The lookbehind now excludes `.` as well.

The three worst-affected documents are ones whose *subject* is a float constant — `0.00555556`
(the `1/180` scaling constant) and `0.00390625` (`1/256`). **A checker that mis-reads the very
constants a formula document exists to record is worst exactly where the documents are best.**

### The part worth more than the bug

**`repos-78` already found this, and reported it** — `_index/_inbox/repos-78.md:222`, *"the regex
matches inside `0.0078125f` … worth a word-boundary tweak that rejects a preceding `.`"*.

**It was never merged, the regex was never changed, and it spread to 22 documents before a second
session rediscovered it independently.**

> **The inbox backlog is not untidiness. It is rediscovery cost, paid in full, twice.** The earlier
> audit of that backlog measured 19 UNMERGED and 74 PARTIAL sections and concluded the merge debt
> was "much smaller than feared". **That conclusion was about volume and this is about value:** one
> unmerged line contained the fix to a defect that was, at that moment, silently corrupting the
> citation gate across the whole database.

`repos-d6` also recorded that it checked `repos-78.md` **before** writing, *"having recommended
something to `repos-c7` earlier tonight without checking"* — a session correcting its own process
in the same report. That is the behaviour the standard asks for and it should be said out loud.

---

## A census invented a mechanism out of its own arithmetic — 2026-09-04

**The coordinator's PKFL census produced a false structural finding, and a session using that census
found it.** Recorded in full because the failure is subtle, the output looked like data, and the
conclusion drawn from it was about to become a scored denominator.

### What the census said

`census_pkfl_container.py` computed each slot's length as **the distance to the next present
offset**. Over 10,314 files it reported:

| pair | even slot median | odd slot median |
|---|---:|---:|
| 8/9 · 10/11 · 12/13 | 2,048 B | **0 B** |

From those zeros the category README concluded: *"the even slot carries the payload and the odd slot
is a small companion — often zero bytes… the **DAT/CTL shape** `repos-4b` established inside EBP,
appearing again one container up."* And from that: **"the scored denominator is 14 pair-units, not
28 slots."**

### What was actually there

**Slots share offsets.** The container sheet re-parsed all 10,314 files: **2,349 have two or more
slots pointing at the same place**, and of 2,403 duplicate groups **2,385 are entirely odd slots,
2,259 of them pointing inside the directory itself.**

In `alc_a0100.snd`, slots 1, 5, 7, 9, 11, 13 and 15 all carry offset `0x40` — **slot 14's own
directory entry.** The bytes there are the pair of directory words read as data: a canonical
*"this half is empty"* marker, cheaper than a null.

> **Under "next offset minus this offset", an aliased slot measures 0 and the last alias in a group
> measures ~1,984 B spanning the gap to the first real payload. Neither is a payload size.** The
> `0 B` medians were the arithmetic describing itself.

### Three separate failures, and only the first is the arithmetic

1. **The census emitted a plausible number for a case it could not measure.** A `0` is not a
   refusal; it reads as a measurement.
2. **A mechanism was inferred from that number** — DAT/CTL — and it was inferred by *analogy to a
   real finding elsewhere*, which made it more persuasive, not less. `repos-4b` had genuinely
   established `CAMDAT`/`CAMCTL` inside EBP. **A true pattern from a neighbouring format is exactly
   the kind of thing that makes a bad reading feel corroborated.**
3. **A scored denominator was proposed on top of it.** The census's one job is to fix a population
   *before* anyone writes against it — and it came within one careful re-parse of fixing the wrong
   one.

### What survives

*"Every occupied slot has its even/odd partner"* is **true, 10,314 / 10,314** — but **partly an
artefact of the aliasing rather than of every partner carrying data.** The invariant as counted is
correct; what it implies about content is weaker than it looks. **The 14-pair denominator is
withdrawn and PKFL is not scoreable until the container sheet's questions are settled.**

Two further corrections from the same re-parse: **offsets are not ascending in slot order** (unlike
EBP), and **the first payload is not at the directory end** — in 2,268 files the smallest offset is
`0x40`, *before* the 16-slot directory ends at `0x48`.

### The instrument was fixed, not just the document

`slots()` now returns `None` for any slot sharing an offset, and the census prints
`ALIASED (length unmeasurable)`. **Correcting the README alone would have left the generator able to
produce the same artefact for the next reader.**

> **A census that emits a plausible number for a case it cannot measure is worse than one that
> refuses, because the number gets read.**

### The pattern across three coordinator errors in one afternoon

| error | how it was caught |
|---|---|
| *"0 never present — a DENSE directory"* | the range stopped at the highest **occupied** index, so it could not contain an empty one — false by construction |
| `0 B` medians → DAT/CTL → 14-pair denominator | a session re-parsed the same 10,314 files independently |
| the `0x` citation fix left a decimal over-count | a session measured the *other* direction on its own sheet |

**Not one was caught because the result looked wrong.** Each was caught by checking an instrument
against a case whose answer was already known — a control, a second parse, an opposite measurement.
**The results all looked entirely reasonable, which is the point.**

---

## Instruments that lied — enemy-record round, merged 2026-09-07

### A proposal id named two different questions, and one of them was already merged

`section-002-text.md` cited `PROPOSED-repos-3f-1` for *"what does entry bit 16 select"*. That
question **had already been merged, on 2026-09-03, as `BX-1`** — but the merge assigned the id in
`OPEN-QUESTIONS.md` and **never rewrote the citation in the document**. Three days later the same
session filed a second round and reused `-1` for an unrelated question about `.ard` slot-1 coverage.

> **Two different questions, one id, and the document still pointing at the dead one.**

**The proposal scheme has no round component**, so a session that files twice reuses `-1`, `-2`, `-3`.
Nothing detects it: `check_questions.py` validates assigned ids, and a `PROPOSED-*` string is
invisible to it by design.

**Two rules, and the second is the cheaper one:**

1. **A merge is not complete until the citing documents carry the assigned id.** Grep the whole
   database for the proposal id as the last step of every merge, not the first.
2. **A `PROPOSED-*` id surviving in a document is itself the defect signal** — it means either the
   question was never merged, or it was merged and the citation was left behind. Either way the
   document is pointing at something that does not exist. Worth a checker.

Fixed at merge: `section-002-text.md` now cites `BX-1`; the new question is `CB-1`.

### A clamp extractor tracked register immediates by last-write and got four constants wrong

`repos-c7`'s extractor reported output `+0x13` as **999** (really **3**, from `mov r9d,3`) and
`+0x0D`–`+0x12` as **99** (really **100**, from `lea r15d,[r9+0x61]` with `r9 = 3`).

**A `lea` used as arithmetic is invisible to a last-write immediate tracker**, and `lea r15d,[r9+0x61]`
is exactly the idiom MSVC emits for *"a constant plus a register that already holds a constant."*

Caught by hand-reading before publication. **The corrected values then matched the existing sheet
exactly — and the agreement is what confirmed the fix, rather than the fix being taken on trust.**

### A byte-presence scan matched the REX.W prefix and reported a perfect score

`repos-d6`'s scan reported **305/305**. `0x48` is both the byte being looked for and the REX.W
prefix on almost every 64-bit instruction in the image, so the scan was matching the encoding of the
instructions rather than the operand it meant to find.

> **A perfect score on a byte-level scan of x86 is a defect report, not a result.** The prefix bytes
> `0x40`–`0x4F` are present in most instructions; any scan for a single byte in that range measures
> the instruction stream, not the data.

A separate noisy read scan by the same session was overturned by a write scan. **Both instrument
failures were caught pre-publication** — which is the outcome the discipline is for.

### A summary was one clause stronger than its source, for the second time in three days

The work board's *"already established"* block said a definition record's `+0x004` resolves
**"→ a row in one of the four `.ard` st2e tables."** `authored-data-table-registry.md`, which the
block cited, states the lookup **generically** and **never says `.ard`**.

`BattleStats_RebuildEffectiveStatsAndAffinityState` does that lookup **twice against two different
registry slots**, and `0x02EBF130` — *character/unit growth definitions* — is not an `.ard` slot at
all. **Two authors were building on the wrong half.**

**The source document was correct.** The error was introduced by the summary of it, which is the
identical failure the 2026-09-06 board rewrite had been written to fix.

> **A summary that reads as settled is the most dangerous artefact in this project**, because it is
> what a session under time pressure reads instead of the source. Cite the document; do not restate
> its conclusion in stronger words.

### A session's own name is an observation, not a constant

`ListAgents` returned `repos-4b` to the session that was actually `repos-3f`; hours of output landed
in another session's inbox, board row, scratch directory and **audit stamps**. Under the corrected
identity the session had audited and stamped **two sheets it wrote itself**.

**Re-check the session name before every write that carries attribution** — an inbox path, a board
row, a `last_pass_by` stamp. The cost of being wrong is not a mislabelled file; it is an audit that
looks independent and is not.

### `ebp_explained`'s denominator and the dispatcher disagree by exactly one

The census measures **13 section types over 1,115 shipped files**. The dispatcher has **14 live
slots** — `section_017` has a handler (`FUN_00332110` → `FUN_003DFE60(1, ptr)`) and **zero users in
any shipped file**.

**Neither number is wrong.** The census measures files and the dispatcher measures code, and they
are answering different questions. **But the difference should be stated where the denominator is
defined**, rather than left for the next session to rediscover as an apparent defect. Tracked as
`CD-3`.

### A hand-counted body size assumed a displacement byte that was not there

`what-the-console-tunes.md` carried `00200BE0` as **8 B**. It is **7**.

```
83 39 06     cmp dword ptr [rcx], 6      <- what is actually encoded
83 79 08 06  cmp dword ptr [rcx+8], 6    <- what the count assumed
```

**One ModRM `disp8` separates them**, and 4+3+1 = 8 is exactly the number that reached the document.

> **A `disp8` slip corrupts an offset claim as well as a size claim.** Getting the size wrong is
> cosmetic; concluding the tag lives at `+8` when it lives at `+0` is not. Here the document never
> stated an offset, so only the size was published — but the same miscount in a document that *does*
> state offsets would have been invisible to `audit_crossrefs.py`, which checks sizes and not
> operand forms.

**Count body sizes by measuring, never by encoding from memory.** The expectation was reasonable —
every sibling accessor in this family (`Variant_ReadFloat`, `Variant_ReadInt`) does take a
displacement, because they read a tag off a field. `Variant_IsAbsent` is the one that reads it at
offset 0.

### A reconciled column used as a filter makes the test prove itself

`scriptvm-verb-table.tsv` carries three related fields: `cat_args` (the ff12-script catalog's
argument count), `pops_int`/`pops_float` (measured from the handler body), and **`arity`** — which is
**neither**. `arity` is *reconciled*: equal to both when they agree, equal to **`cat_args`** when they
do not.

Filtering on `pops_int + pops_float == arity` and then testing `arity == cat_args` therefore selects
exactly the rows where the two already agreed. It reported **567 of 567, zero disagreements.** The
true figure is **575 of 645 — 70 disagreements.**

> **The tell was the perfection.** A question open for days does not close at 100.0 % on the first
> query. **When a result is too clean, look for the join that removed the counterexamples** — not for
> confirmation that it is right.

**The same shape appears in the 2026-09-02 falsification pass** on the same document — *"arity over
all 1,224 rows shows no shift in any band"*, over a population selected the same way. **It reached
the correct answer by a route that could not have reached an incorrect one.** The answer stands
because it was re-derived without the reconciled column; the method does not.

**Rule: never filter on a column that was computed from the two things you are comparing.** Prefer
the two raw measurements, and if only a reconciled column exists, say what it reconciles before using
it.

**Scope, measured 2026-09-07 rather than assumed.** *"`arity` takes the catalog's value on
disagreement"* is true **of the `cat_known` population — 95 of 96 rows** — which is the population
the test used, so the circularity is real. It is **false in general**: over all family-0 rows,
`arity` equals the measurement in 285 disagreement cases and the catalog in 95, because for
catalog-unknown verbs `cat_args` is a meaningless 0 and the measurement wins. **The rule needed its
scope stated, and stating it required counting rather than reading ten rows.**

### `cat_args = 0` is the ff12-script catalog's default for a verb it does not know

334 family-0 rows have `cat_args != arity`; **333 of them have `cat_args == 0`**, and they are the
rows where `cat_known` is false. **A catalog default and a genuine zero-argument verb are
indistinguishable in that column.**

Any comparison against `cat_args` must be scoped to `cat_known == True` first, or it manufactures
hundreds of disagreements out of absent data. Scoping it that way leaves 645 real anchors.

### Counting `Pop*` calls does not measure a script verb's argument count

Three separate instruments were tried on the same question and **all three are wrong in different
directions**:

| instrument | fails when | direction |
|---|---|---|
| `pops_int + pops_float` | the handler **pushes its own operand and pops it back** | over-counts |
| `pops − pushes` | the handler **pushes a return value** (`ames` nets −1, `messync` −2) | under-counts |
| the reconciled `arity` column | it takes the catalog's value on disagreement, in `cat_known` rows | circular |

The push-then-pop idiom is real and shared: `0034C290` is the `begin` callback for **three**
different `*sync` verbs and consists of `PushInt32(vs, 0)` followed immediately by
`PopInt32CoerceFloat32(vs)`.

> **A `Pop` is not an argument.** It is an argument only if nothing in the same body pushed it, and
> a `Push` is not an argument either — it may be the return value. **Arity is a dataflow question
> and was being answered with a histogram.**

**What saved the conclusion was that the conclusion did not depend on the metric.** The index-mapping
test compares the *same* metric at record N against the catalog at record N and asks whether
agreement collapses under a shift. Run with the over-counting metric it gives 89.1 % against a
56.0 % shifted baseline; with the under-counting metric, 97.2 % against 59.0 %. **When a result must
survive a broken instrument, make it a comparison rather than a measurement.**

### A claim inferred from a count, killed by reading four bodies

*"The extra operand is the handle of the thing being waited on"* was written into a falsification
pass, a register entry and an audit-log entry on 2026-09-07, on the strength of a delta histogram
and the names of five verbs. **The handlers push their own zero.** There is no handle.

**The cost of the shortcut was one decompile run of 29 functions, which took six seconds.** The
count told me *that* something was there; only the body said *what*. **Nothing in the count could
have distinguished "pops an implicit argument" from "pops what it just pushed" — and the second is
the more common idiom in this table.**

### `0016D6A0` is the CFG check function *and* an ICF-folded `ret` stub — both names were true

`check_consistency` flagged one address described three ways across five citations:
`ICF_FoldedRetStub` in three documents, `_guard_check_icall` and `set_name` in a fourth. Settled from
the authorized EXE rather than by picking a side:

```
0016D6A0:  C2 00 00        ret 0          (3 bytes, then int3 padding)

PE load config (rva 0x1BCBE40, size 148):
  GuardCFCheckFunctionPointer    = 0x008C31C0   ->  *ptr = 0x0016D6A0
  GuardCFDispatchFunctionPointer = 0x008C31C8
  GuardCFFunctionTable           = 0            count = 0
  GuardFlags                     = 0x00000100   (IMAGE_GUARD_CF_INSTRUMENTED only)
```

**The shipping build is CFG-instrumented and CFG is inert.** The compiler emitted
`_guard_check_icall` call sites throughout the image — one turns up in a ScriptVM poll handler — but
there is **no valid-target table** and the check function is a bare return.

> **Two documents were not contradicting each other; they were describing the same three bytes from
> two directions.** Identical-COMDAT folding collapses every bare-`ret` body onto one address, and
> the CFG check is one of them. **A name collision on a folded stub is the expected outcome of ICF,
> not a defect** — but the database must still say it in one voice, because a reader looking up
> `0016D6A0` gets three answers and no way to tell they are compatible.

**Bears on `B-2/a`.** *"Which empty destructors the linker folded onto `0016D6A0`"* now has a
structural half-answer: anything whose body is `ret 0` folds here. That does not recover the
source-level identities, and it does explain the population.

**Method note:** the disagreement was found by `check_consistency` and resolved by reading the PE
load-config directory — **eight bytes of header settled an argument that five documents could not.**
Where two documents name one address differently, prefer the structure that *defines* the name over
the structure that merely *contains* it.

### `iat_xrefs.py` undercounts every import reached through a jump thunk — by an order of magnitude

Found by `xiionline-8d`, 2026-09-07, and it invalidates any *"who calls X"* claim built on that
script. It counts only direct `FF 15` call-through-IAT and never follows MSVC's forwarder chains.

Measured against the authorized EXE:

| import | `iat_xrefs.py` said | actual |
|---|---:|---:|
| `sqrtf` | **0** | **566** |
| `memset` | **0** | **830** |
| `memcpy` | **0** | **632** |
| `free` | 37 | **860** |

The headline moves from *"417 of 538 slots, 3,543 sites"* to **495 of 538 slots, 7,707 sites** once
`E9` / `FF 25` forwarder chains are followed transitively. **MSVC nests them up to three deep** —
`00809950` → `00210EB0` → `0019C590` → IAT.

> **The near-miss is the instructive part.** The session was about to publish *"the game ships its
> own maths library because it never calls `sinf`"* — a conclusion drawn from a zero. **`memset`
> scoring zero is what exposed it**, because no C++ program of this size calls `memset` zero times.

**A zero from a scanner is a claim about the scanner until a positive control says otherwise.** Pick
a control the answer cannot possibly be zero for, and run it first. Corrected walker in
`Tools/work/xiionline-8d/`.

### `check_consistency.py` cross-pairs every address with every name in one table row

`00902FD0` was reported as named both `BegineSceneObj` and `EndSceneObj` by the same document. It is
not a defect and it is not a function — it is a `.rdata` **vtable**, correctly named in that document
twice. The trigger is a single summary row listing a **pair**:

```
| `00902FB0` / `00902FD0` | `BegineSceneObj` / `EndSceneObj` vtables | `.rdata` | |
```

The extractor pairs every address in the row with every name in the row, so a legitimate two-column
pairing manufactures a disagreement.

**Writing rule: one address, one name, one row.** A row that lists a pair reads well and defeats the
only checker that guards naming. Split it, or state the pairing in prose.

### A library inventory built from tag names is a census of the tags, not of the binary

`third-party-middleware-inventory.md` cited **GFZ tag names** as its evidence — *"four GFZ-tagged
MDCT/window functions under `Audio.Tremor`"*. Tag names are a prior session's claim about the binary
at today's error rate. Tested against the image, the inventory split three ways:

| claim | verdict | what decided it |
|---|---|---|
| Tremor and libvorbis both ship | **CONFIRMED** | Tremor **0** float-SSE ops in 5,791 B; libvorbis **121** in 7,498 B |
| Bullet, JsonBox, Dear ImGui, YEBIS ship | **CONFIRMED** | own symbols, genuine RTTI, authored filenames, licence strings |
| libxml2 ships | **NOT SUPPORTED** | six markers, zero hits; `xmlStrchr` is a generic `strchr`; the name appears **0** times in the binary |

**Two opposite lessons in one pass, and taking either alone would have produced a wrong answer.**

> **String absence is not absence.** The image has no `Xiph.Org`, no `libVorbis`, no `vorbis_` — one
> `OggS` page magic. **A decoder is tables and arithmetic and can be entirely string-free.** A pass
> that stopped at the string scan would have refuted a true claim.
>
> **A body that every C library shares identifies nothing.** `002B39A0` really is `strchr` — and
> that is exactly why calling it `xmlStrchr` is unfalsifiable from the body. **The test that
> separated Tremor from libvorbis worked because float-versus-integer is a property the tag does not
> encode**; the test that would separate libxml2's `strchr` from anyone else's does not exist.

**Rule: verify a library by a property its own name could not have produced.** Float-versus-integer,
a version banner, a genuine RTTI descriptor, an own-namespace symbol. Never by a body shape shared
across the ecosystem, and never by the tag that is the thing under test.

### `0016D6A0` has three proven roles, and the ledger's name is false at two of them

Recorded 2026-09-07 after `xiionline-13` found the third. The folded `ret 0` body serves:

1. **The Control Flow Guard check** — named by the PE load config.
2. **At least one array-element destructor** — named by `Unwind@0089dd0e`.
3. **A retail-stubbed frame-phase marker** — call sites are `lea rcx,[string]; call 0016D6A0`,
   recovering `PAD CONTROL`, `DRAW`, `CAMERA`, `FAST MODE`, `VFX`, `UI VFX`, `ATEL CONTROL`,
   `SOUND`, `CLOSE FULLMENU`, `SCREEN CAPTURE`, `CAPTURE DRAW`, plus a `[PS2]*` family.

**The name `_guard_check_icall` is the GFZ ledger's own**, not this database's invention — it appears
in decompiler output. **It is false at roles 2 and 3.** Ghidra therefore renders *the engine naming
its own frame phases* as `_guard_check_icall("PAD CONTROL")`.

> **Read every `_guard_check_icall(...)` in decompiled output as "a call to the folded no-op", and
> let the argument say which role it is** — a string pointer is a phase marker, an integer is
> something else. **This is `/OPT:ICF` damage beyond a missing name: it silently attributes one
> library function's identity to unrelated engine call sites.**

### A spin loop cannot be verified from decompiled output at all

`CF-6` originally said decompiled threading claims owe a re-derivation. The truth is sharper, and it
has **two failure modes pointing in opposite directions**:

```asm
0036F2C0  ; GENUINE — re-reads inside the loop
   mov ecx,[02EBA3F8] / mov eax,[02EBA3F8] / cmp / jne back-to-the-first-load

0036F2F0  ; INERT — the load is hoisted out
   mov eax,[02EAF6D8] / and eax,0x100 / test eax,eax / jne back-to-the-TEST
```

**Thirty bytes apart, and they are opposites.** The second spins on a register nothing inside the
loop can change, so it either never runs or never exits **regardless of who writes the word**.

> **Only the branch target separates a working spin from a dead one, and the branch target is exactly
> what decompilation discards.** The decompiler **deletes** genuine spins (CSE folds the two loads)
> and **preserves** inert ones as if they were real. Both render as ordinary loops.

**Verify a spin by disassembling it and asking where the branch goes — never by reading its
operands.**

### "All references are loads" does not distinguish a producer from dead code

`xiionline-13` named a sound killer observation — *"is `02EAF6D8` written by frame code?"* — censused
all 17 sites, found every reference to be a load, and read that as **confirming** a cross-thread
producer. For a **hoisted** read, "all references are loads" is equally consistent with the loop being
dead.

**The observation was sound; the inference was not.** Re-attacking the same bodies for their **branch
targets** rather than their **operands** is what broke it — and what broke it was the author, on a
claim they had already reported as having survived.

### `UNW_FLAG_CHAININFO` manufactures a false gameplay-parent population

The banned-evidence class, in a new disguise, and one command away. Walking
`.pdata` → `UNW_FLAG_CHAININFO` → chained `RUNTIME_FUNCTION` → "parent" yields **3,828 parents**
owned by Movement 127, ActorEntity 54, CombatAction 49, Script-VM 34, CombatFormula 21, CombatAI 20,
Party 16, Spawn 12 — **which reads exactly like a refutation of `B-2`'s "not one gameplay parent".**

Two measurements kill it:

- **Zero** of 11,353 CHAININFO entries chain to a parent registering `__CxxFrameHandler3`.
- **All 11,339 resolvable ones chain to the function they are already inside** — same-function
  11,339, different-function **0**.

**`CHAININFO` in this binary means only "this code region of function X shares X's unwind info".**
Every "parent" is its own parent; there is no parent relation to read. Nor is it PGO hot/cold
splitting — the chunks sit beside and inside their parents (median 62 B, median distance 154) and
their address-band histogram mirrors the parents' exactly.

> **This is precisely why `.pdata` is banned as semantic evidence.** The prior branch that lost 182
> classifications did not make an obvious mistake; it ran a standard, documented traversal and
> believed the output. **A structure that legitimately describes code layout will answer a semantic
> question with confident nonsense.**

### Body sizes written from memory, and the gate that cannot catch them

Three cited body sizes in a draft were off by **61, 98 and 8 bytes** — written from recollection
rather than re-measured. `check_consistency` compares documents against **each other**, so a wrong
size is invisible until a *second* document disagrees.

**The only defence is re-measuring every cited address against the function list before the gate
runs.** A size is a measurement; there is no such thing as remembering one.

### `check_source_hygiene.py` takes `--paths`, not `--db`

Every other checker in the kit takes `--db`. This one errors out on it, so a gate block copied from
the standard set fails at exactly the step meant to catch stray bytes. Fixed in the session briefs.

### An absence claim about the DATABASE needs a search of the database

**Two sessions made this mistake independently within one hour on 2026-09-07**, which is what makes it
structural rather than careless.

| session | claim | reality | the check that would have caught it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `xiionline-8d` | `001D0390` references **301 developer debug-menu strings** shipped in retail and *"documented nowhere in the database"* | It is the **label-table installer of the Developer-Tuning-Console**, stated at tier 1 in `arm-catalogue-slice-E-ids-140-171.md` §4 since 2026-09-03. The strings are the console's **293 command labels**. | `grep -rn 001D0390` — **six seconds** |
| `xiionline-61` | **40 registry slots** have *"a named consumer sitting there unused"* | **39 of the 40 were already cited** by a document. Exactly **one** — slot 14, `02EBF078` — has a named reader and no citation. | grep each slot address across the database — **seconds** |

> **An absence claim about the database needs a search of the database, exactly as an absence claim
> about the binary needs a positive control.** Both sessions had been rigorous about the second all
> day and neither applied it to the first.

**The failure mode is specific and worth naming: a discovery does not prompt its own verification.**
A measurement invites the question *"is my instrument right?"* A discovery invites the question
*"what does this mean?"* — and never *"is this already known?"* Both sessions ran positive controls
on their scanners and neither ran a grep on the answer.

**Consequence for the register:** `CH-2` was **struck as filed in error**, not closed as answered.
Recording it as answered would put a discovery in the ledger that never happened. The same applies to
any question whose premise turns out to describe existing work.

### A scanner reports what its window landed on; only a decoder reports what the program does

Third, fourth and fifth instances in one session, all from `xiionline-8d`:

| scanner | reported | decoding showed |
|---|---|---|
| `FF 15`-only import census | `sqrtf` **0** sites | **566** |
| `0F 51/52/53` byte pattern | 18 `sqrtps`, 4 `rcpps` | **13**, and **0** |
| displacement string scan | **301** "debug menu strings" | **293** documented label stores |

**The third is the worst of the three, and not because the number is furthest out.** It is the worst
because it looked like a **discovery** rather than a measurement, and nothing about a discovery
prompts you to check whether it is already known.

### A census that measures the binary and not the database re-finds what is already known

The registry census disassembled all of `.text` and attributed every reference into
`02EBF008`-`02EBF1F8`. Sound method, correct output, and **the conclusion drawn from it was wrong**,
because the interesting quantity was never *"which slots have named readers"* (40) but *"which slots
the database has not written about"* (1).

**Measured against the binary, 40 looked like a worklist. Measured against the database, it was a
confirmation of prior work.** The real gap — **46 slots cited, 9 explained** — is a *depth* problem
that no scan closes.

> **Before publishing a worklist, subtract what is already written.** Otherwise the instrument
> measures the engine and the claim is about the database, and those are different denominators.

### The novelty overclaim survives *partial* reading — a third instance, one hour later

`xiionline-13` applied the absence-claim rule to their own earlier report within a minute of reading
it, and it caught a live error: they had told the coordinator that `gi-render-command-family.md`
contains *"36 three-slot command classes it never mentions, including the two it does own."*
Searching the document for all 36 names returns **two** hits. **It does mention the two it owns, and
the number is 34.**

**Their diagnosis is sharper than the rule I wrote, and it is the part to keep:**

> **The failure survives partial reading.** They had opened that document, quoted its four shapes,
> and cited its device-vtable slots in their own §5. **Having read *some* of a document is what made
> them confident enough not to search it. A stranger document would have got the grep.**

That is the third instance in two hours, and the mechanism differs each time: `xiionline-8d` never
opened the document; this session never checked the database at all; `xiionline-13` had read part of
the document and generalised from the part they read. **Familiarity is the risk factor, not
ignorance.**

**And the framing mattered as much as the number.** *"Never mentions"* implies an omission; the
document's census is **correct within its own stated scope** (18 `Gi*` classes) and the 34 are a gap
in a neighbouring vtable block. The original wording would have sent the next reader hunting a
mistake that does not exist. **State a scope gap as a scope gap.**

**The search also produced two corroborations nobody was looking for:** the document already records
*"three-slot classes : 2"*, the same contract derived independently from the other 34; and it already
cites `GeometryInstanceOrderedObject` (vtable `00903920`) at its §16, so both documents had touched
the `OrderedObject` family without either knowing it. **Searching before claiming does not only
prevent errors — it finds the corroboration that assuming skips past.**

### A correction needs its own control

`the-maths-the-game-runs-on.md` reported a byte-pattern scan finding 18 `sqrtps` / 14 `rsqrtps` /
4 `rcpps`, then a disassembly pass cutting those to 13 / 10 / **0** — an honest, explicit instrument
note about a 28 % false-positive rate.

**The disassembly pass removed two true positives along with the false ones.** An independent decode
of every function body finds **`rcpps` = 2** — both real, in `Runtime.Math.VectorPowf4` (`0080A270`),
in a clean 1,191-of-1,191-byte decode with diagnostic context (`psrlq xmm, 0x14` exponent extraction,
`rcpps` → `cvtps2pd` reciprocal seed widened to double). `sqrtps` is **14**, not 13.

> **The scan was checked against a decoder; the decoder was checked against nothing.** Fixing a
> false-positive problem introduced false negatives, and those are harder to see because the number
> moved in the direction the author already expected. **When a fix moves a count toward the answer
> you predicted, that is the moment to re-measure, not the moment to stop.**

### The same document proved CFG inert by reading a body, then accepted field-presence for the cookie

`image-hardening-cfg-aslr-dep-and-stack-cookies.md` carried *"stack cookies are live"* at **tier 1**,
evidenced by **`SecurityCookie = 0x01F44830` appears in the load config.**

**That is the presence of a field, not proof anything uses it — the identical evidential shape that
made CFG look enforced.** The document had already proved CFG inert *by reading the check body*, and
then two paragraphs later accepted a weaker argument for a neighbouring claim.

> **The standard was available in the same document and was not applied to the claim beside it.**
> Reading one body does not raise the tier of the claims around it. Every mechanism in a posture
> table needs its own body read, because a posture table is exactly where a reader will assume
> uniform evidence.

Read end to end, the cookie **is** live — `__security_init_cookie` mixes five entropy sources and
masks to 48 bits, `__security_check_cookie` is 33 bytes of real comparison with **2,209 callers** —
so the claim survived. **It survived by luck of being true, not by the evidence offered for it.**

### "Untuned" and "unhardened" are different properties

The prior behind `B-2/f` was that `/fp:precise` plus no app-local CRT reads as an untuned build, so
the rest of the hardening was probably nominal too. **It is not.** CFG is the only nominal protection;
ASLR is off *deliberately* (relocations shipped, `RELOCS_STRIPPED` clear, `.reloc` `0x39360` bytes —
a build that had merely forgotten ASLR usually strips them), DEP is enforced, cookies are live.

**A build-configuration inference is a hypothesis about a person, not a measurement of a binary.**
It suggested where to look and was wrong about what would be found.

### A row splice keyed on a generic delimiter deleted the row beside it

Replacing the `B-2/f` register row programmatically, the edit cut from the row's start to *"the end
of the line containing the next `| OPEN |`"*. **`| OPEN |` is the status cell of almost every row in
that table**, so the cut ran past the intended row and **silently deleted `B-2/b`.**

`check_questions.py` caught it on the next run — *"ids cited by a document with NO row in the table:
1 (B-2/b)"* — because a document still cited the id. **The gate did exactly what it exists for**, and
the row was restored from its original text rather than re-written from memory.

> **Never anchor a structural edit on a delimiter the format repeats.** Anchor on the row's own id,
> and assert the match count before writing. **The failure is silent by construction: a deleted row
> leaves no trace in the file it was deleted from** — only a checker that reads *other* files can see
> it.

### A confirmed count is not a checked claim

An independent audit of `the-maths-the-game-runs-on.md` corrected `sqrtps` 13 -> 14 and `rcpps` 0 -> 2,
noted in passing that **all fourteen `sqrtps` sites are `Audio`-owned**, and passed the document.

**The larger error was in the counts the audit *confirmed*.** Pulling the same thread across the whole
set: `rsqrtps` **10 / 10 Audio**, `sqrtss` **3 / 4 Audio**. Every SIMD square root in the image belongs
to the sound engine — including the `rsqrtps` group the document's §2 and §4 were built around and
presented as **geometry**.

The bodies were read correctly; 3-D positional audio genuinely needs cross products and normalises.
**The attribution was wrong, and it would have sent a modder hunting geometry code into the audio
subsystem.**

> **Confirming a number feels like finishing with it.** *"14 is really 14"* prompts nothing about what
> the fourteen **are**. **An audit that verifies figures and not attributions will pass a document
> whose figures are all correct and whose reading is inverted.**

The corrected claim is *stronger*: the geometry path contains **no SIMD at all** and takes every square
root through the 566 scalar `sqrtf` calls, which is exactly what `/fp:precise` predicts. Two headline
findings that merely sat next to each other now corroborate each other.

### A REX fix that created a REX blind spot in the same instruction family

The scan behind those counts excluded any `0F 5x` whose preceding byte was `0x40`-`0x4F` — **an
exclusion added to fix an earlier REX undercount**. It then discarded the REX-prefixed **NP-forms**:
`44 0F 51` at `007F0A17`, `44 0F 53` at `0080A339`, `45 0F 53` at `0080A33D`.

**The fix for one blind spot created another, in the same family, on the same line** — and because the
corrected count moved *toward* the expected answer it read as confirmation.

**The replacement is not a better scan; it is no scan.** Disassembling all 38,161 functions linearly
from their own entries takes seconds and reproduces 14 / 10 / 4 / 2 with no correction step at all.
**When a scanner needs a correction table, the decoder was probably always affordable.**

### A deletion is invisible in the file it happened to

Every instrument error recorded this session left **a wrong value someone could read and doubt**. The
`B-2/b` row-splice left **nothing** — the row was gone, the table still parsed, and the file gave no
sign. It surfaced only because a *different* file still cited the id and `check_questions.py` reads
across files.

> **A programmatic anchor must be unique, not merely correct at the point you looked.** Anchoring on
> `| OPEN |` — the status cell of nearly every row — was correct where it was tested and wrong two
> rows later. Assert the match count before writing.

### A typed struct in the GFZ names one record two different ways, inside one function

Decompiling `BattleStats_RebuildEffectiveStatsAndAffinityState` renders it against a `target_bkbi`
struct with names like `nStrengthModifier` and `nWeaponGuardRateModifier`. **The function refutes
them.** It has two branches on `rec[+0x05]`, and the *same output slot* is fed by differently-named
fields on each:

| output slot | `rec[+0x05] == 0` | otherwise | clamp |
|---|---|---|---:|
| A | `(float)nAttackPowerModifier` | `… + nStrengthModifier` | **99** |
| B | `(float)nMagickResistModifier` | `… + nMagickPowerModifier` | **99** |
| C | `(float)nWeaponGuardRateModifier` | `… + nVitalityModifier` | **99** |
| D | `(float)nSpecialGuardRateModifier` | `… + nSpeedModifier` | **99** |

**All four clamp at 99** — the cap proved *from readers* to belong to Strength, Magick Power,
Vitality and Speed. So on the character path, four fields named after Attack, Magick Resist and two
guard rates are read as floats and become the four core attributes.

> **A decompiler struct is a prior session's claim wearing the typography of a fact.** These names
> arrive rendered into the C as though the binary asserted them, and nothing in the output marks them
> as an annotation. **The inconsistency is invisible unless you read both branches** — and a reader
> who opens the function at the branch they care about will find perfectly plausible names.

**Rule: never quote a decompiler-supplied field name as evidence.** Treat it exactly as a GFZ
comment — a lead to check against a reader, never a source. Where a struct name and a clamp class
disagree, **the clamp is a machine fact and the name is not.**

### Searching for a name in the form you expect it to be written

**Two sessions produced the same wrong answer from the same cause, hours apart.** A Phyre document
concluded 22 classes were absent from the image; an auditor's first attempt at refuting it found only
2 of 22 and was about to file a narrow partial correction.

**Phyre does not emit RTTI for a class.** It emits RTTI for `PClassDescriptor*<TheClass>`, so the
class name appears **as a template argument**:

```
.?AV?$PClassDescriptorAbstract@VPShaderVertexProgram@PRendering@Phyre@@@Phyre@@
```

Searching for `.?AVPShaderVertexProgram@Phyre@@` finds almost nothing. Searching for the name *as an
argument* finds everything — **22/22 by bare string, 21/22 by descriptor RTTI, 21/22 by script
accessor.**

> **The absence was an artefact of the query's shape, not a fact about the binary.** Both readers
> knew the class name; neither anticipated the form it is written in.

**The control is what escaped it, and it is cheap:** of 18 classes the document says *are* recovered,
only **one** has a direct RTTI descriptor, and the image holds **40** `@Phyre@@` descriptors against
**246** class names. **A search that finds 1 of 18 known positives cannot support a negative about
anything.** Run the query against the things you already know are there, before believing what it
says is missing.

### The one claim whose procedure was unstated is the one that was wrong

The same document specifies its corpus scanner reproducibly in §5 — sections, bounds, tool. **Its
image-side string search in §3b states no method at all**, and that is the claim that failed.

> **Every other claim in the document is reproducible from what it writes down. The single
> unreproducible one is the single wrong one.**

An observed correlation inside one document, which is a stronger argument for recording method than
the principle asserted in the abstract. **A falsified measurement with no recorded procedure cannot
be debugged, only replaced** — the auditor could show it was wrong and could not show why.

### A permissive matcher announces itself by making a residual disappear

Two sessions censused the same 22 Phyre classes for script accessors. One got **22/22**, the other
**21/22**. The looser regex matched the class name anywhere inside the angle brackets, so it counted

```
PArray<class Phyre::PRendering::PContextSwitch const *,8>::Get
```

— an accessor for **an array of** the class, not for the class itself.

**The strict count is right, and the difference is one row.** That row is the entire content of an
open question: *"is `PContextSwitch` genuinely unregistered, or written in a third form?"*

> **A permissive matcher does not usually announce itself by producing a wrong answer.** It announces
> itself by producing a *tidier* one — the residual vanishes, the census reads 22/22, and the question
> that would have been asked is never written down. **Suspect a matcher most when it closes the last
> gap.**

### Attribution is evidence, not courtesy

An audit-log entry recorded a refuted document as belonging to the session that applied its REOPEN.
**It was written two days earlier by someone else**, and that session raised the correction itself.

This database's own rule is that **the path to a wrong answer is evidence about where the same mistake
is hiding elsewhere.** That rule only works if the trail names the right author: an unstated-method
failure is a lead about *that author's other work*, and attributing it to whoever happened to inherit
the file points every future reader at the wrong body of work.

### A global time base offered as a local knob

A freshly written document's modding notes named `01DFE0B8` — *"one float, no code change"* — as **the
single cheapest lever** on world-loot lifetime. The arithmetic was right: it does multiply the whole
ladder, and `0.5` really would double how long loot survives.

**It is the 60 fps frame-rate scalar.** One of a triple written once at startup by `FUN_001EDDC0`, and
read **438 times across the image** (`01DFE0B4` 211, `01DFE0B8` 45, `01DFE0BC` 182). Changing it to
lengthen loot rescales **every timed system in the game**.

> **A modding note is a claim about consequence, not about arithmetic**, and it is the one kind of
> claim in these documents that a reader will act on directly. *"This float multiplies the term"* is a
> measurement; *"this is the cheapest lever"* is an assertion about everything else that reads it —
> **and that requires a read census, which had not been run.**

**Before naming any address as a lever, count its readers.** The same census that settles what a
global *is* also settles whether it can be moved.

### The same global, censused twice from two categories, one year apart in method

`K-8` (Movement, 2026-09-02) reached this triple from the movement side and reported
**211 / 46 / 182** reads with all three writes inside `001EDDC0`. This pass reached it from the loot
side and got **211 / 45 / 182**, plus the branch that decides the values.

**Two categories, two methods, two sessions, one function — and a one-count difference on the middle
term that neither method's write-up is detailed enough to adjudicate.** Recorded rather than
resolved. The agreement on the structure is the finding; the disagreement on one read is a reminder
that a census is an instrument reading, not a fact.

### Hedging can be an error in its own right

An audit of the PS2-GS frame document recommended making its GIFtag section **less** tentative. The
document apologised for deriving the register field meanings from arithmetic rather than a hardware
table — and the binary proves them three independent ways with no external reference: the record
layout *is* the A+D layout, eleven consecutive valid register ids appear in initialisation order, and
two data words are written in shapes that are nonsense under any other field reading.
