## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — `R-7`, and a data-table blind spot that appeared twice in one block

**Block: `23-Third-Party-Libraries` (debt 200)** — chosen by rebalancing after three consecutive
blocks in `01`. The category's inventory lists **Lua 5.2.1 at 111 functions** with the `lua_pcall`
sites marked *not read*, and the sharpest question there is whether the embedded Lua is live at all.
The trail led to `R-7`, open since the Lua-bindings document was written.
`embedded-lua-and-phyre-object-bindings.md` `audit.passes` 1 → 2. Questions closed 364 → 365.

### `R-7` — the metatable is an identity token, and the evidence for the opposite was a linker artefact

In `.rdata`: `PhyreObject`, then `__index`, `__newindex`, `__eq`, then `m_script`, `m_entryPoint` —
and immediately *before* them, the vtable of `PScripting::PClassCallableMethodScript::PCallerScript`.
**It reads exactly like Phyre installing three metamethods with its own strings around it.**

**It is not.** Those three are entries **0, 1 and 5 of Lua 5.2's `luaT_eventname[]`** at
`01CCBA90`, whose seventeen entries read `__index __newindex __gc __mode __len __eq __add __sub
__mul __div __mod __pow __unm __lt __le __concat __call` — **exact tag-method order**. The linker
scattered that one table's strings across `.rdata` at `00D6FDxx`, `01CCBBxx` and `00D701xx`.

**Reference counts settle it:** `PhyreObject` **13** (the binding layer), `__index` **2** — both
inside Lua, one function tagged `ThirdParty.Scripting.Lua` — `__newindex` **0**, `__eq` **0**.
**Phyre's binding layer names none of them.** The metatable is created, stored under the registry key
and compared with `lua_rawequal`: **an identity token, not a dispatch table**, which is what §4 of the
owning document already said.

**The limit is stated in the answer rather than hidden:** an absent *name-string* reference does not
prove no metamethod is installed, because Lua sets metafields by indexing the event table, not by
naming the string.

> **This is the adjacency rule earning its keep.** Six strings in a row, four owners between them —
> Phyre's class name, Lua's metamethod names, Phyre's field names — and a vtable belonging to none of
> the strings sitting directly above. **Had the proximity been read as meaning, `R-7` would have been
> answered "yes, it carries all three", with a plausible-looking address for each.**

### ⚠ The blind spot, twice in one block

**A code-body scan cannot see a pointer that lives in a data table**, and this block hit that twice:

1. **`00684D10` and `00686280` — the two `lua_pcall` sites with zero callers.** `data_xref_scan.py`
   found **0** references, control passing. They are **vtable slots** at `00D6FDC0`/`00D6FDC8`, found
   only by scanning `.data`/`.rdata` for the raw 8-byte address. **Two further slots of that vtable
   are the folded `005C5230` stub.**
2. **`__index`, `__newindex`, `__eq`.** Code references 2/0/0 — and all three are pointed to from
   `01CCBA90`, which is the Lua event table. **Reading the 0s as "unused" would have been wrong
   twice over**: they are used, by index, by the interpreter.

> **Yesterday the same class of gap was `jmp` thunks; today it is data tables.** The pattern is one
> thing: **"who reaches this?" has as many answers as there are ways to name an address**, and each
> instrument sees one. `data_xref_scan.py` reads code bodies and says so in its docstring; the
> complement — a raw qword search of the data sections — took four lines and was decisive both times.
> **It is now part of the reachability question, not an afterthought:** call, jmp, code operand, data
> pointer.

The qword scan was run with controls — `WorldActorScriptVM_InterpretBytecode` and
`VMNativeCommand_ExecuteLifecycle`, both returning **0** data pointers — so the method does not
simply match everything.

### What else was established

- **Lua is reached entirely through virtual dispatch.** The four executors: two are `PCallerScript`
  vtable slots, two are called from `00685140` / `006853C0` / `00685560`, themselves slots of the
  `PScripting::PScheduler` vtable at `00D6FD68`.
- **No `.lua` file ships.** `lua` is not among the 48 extensions censused over the 89,414-entry
  archive index, so whatever these paths run does not come from shipped data.
- Four Phyre scripting classes named from RTTI: `PAsyncProcessBuffer`, `PScheduler`,
  `PUtilityScripting`, `PClassCallableMethodScript::PCallerScript`.

### Not examined

**Whether anything ever reaches the scheduler at runtime** — the vtables exist and the stubs are
installed, but no caller chain was traced up to game code, so *"Lua is linked and dispatchable"* is
established and *"Lua runs"* is not. That distinction is left open rather than blurred.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — the embedded Lua: linked, instantiated, and probably never fed

**Block: finishing what the previous one deliberately left open.** That block ended with *"'Lua is
linked and dispatchable' is established and 'Lua runs' is not"*, and closing a gap I chose to declare
is better than leaving it for a reader to wonder about. Questions closed 366 → 367.

### The answer, in three levels

**1 — linked.** Lua 5.2.1, 111 functions. Already in the inventory.

**2 — instantiated before `main`, every launch. New, tier 1.** Each of the four Phyre scripting
vtables has a constructor that installs it, and one of them —
**`00166210`, the `PScripting::PUtilityScripting` constructor** — is tagged
`Runtime.StaticLifetimeInfrastructure`, has no callers, and **is entry 516 of the CRT initializer
table at `0x008C31D8`** documented in `static-initialization-order-and-injection-timing.md`.

> **A scripting object exists in every session before the title screen.** This is not dormant
> middleware left behind by a cut feature.
>
> *Instrument check: my parse of that table returned exactly **1,172** entries, the figure the owning
> document states, and the control address is absent from it — so the membership test discriminates.*
> **Building on a documented structure rather than re-deriving it also tested the document**, and it
> held.

**3 — executes a chunk. Not established, and static evidence cannot settle it.**

- **No `.lua` ships** — not among the 48 extensions censused over 89,414 archive entries.
- **Every route into `lua_pcall` is virtual**: two executors are `PCallerScript` vtable slots, two are
  called from three `PScheduler` slots.
- **Neither `PScheduler` nor `PCallerScript` is a static initialiser**, and the one function that
  constructs them, `00680D80`, **is itself a vtable slot** with no direct callers.

> **The chain is virtual at every link.** A breakpoint on `00684D10` answers this in one launch; more
> static work will not. **That is the stopping point, and it is recorded as `DA-1` rather than
> softened into a guess** — the same shape as `AS-1b`, where reflection-driven instantiation was
> explicitly noted as invisible to every static test.
>
> **For a modder: there is a Lua interpreter in the process, and no evidence the shipped game ever
> hands it a script.**

### The method that did the work, twice in two blocks

Every constructor in the table above was found by **searching the data sections for the raw 8-byte
vtable address** — the complement to `data_xref_scan.py`, which reads code bodies. The previous block
established that complement after it hid two `lua_pcall` sites and three metamethod strings; **this
block used it as the first instrument rather than the last**, and it produced the whole constructor
map in one pass.

**The reachability question now has four instruments and they are used together:** `E8 call`,
`E9 jmp`, code memory-operand (`data_xref_scan.py`), and raw data-section pointer. Each of the four
has produced a wrong answer alone in the last two days.

### Not examined

`00166210` itself — **86 bytes, unread** — which decides whether construction creates a `lua_State`
or merely registers the class (`DA-2`). No `lua_load` / `luaL_loadbuffer` call site has been
identified anywhere in this database (`DA-3`); finding one would turn `DA-1` from a runtime question
into a static one. And **two `PCallerScript` vtable slots are the folded `005C5230` stub** — a
removed capability in a scripting-callback table, unidentified (`DA-4`).

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — the subsystem registry, and two corrections to my own work inside one block

Two things, both starting from `DA-2`, an 86-byte read I filed the previous block and could have run
then. Documents 207 → 208; questions closed 367 → 369.

### `DA-2`, and the overstatement it caught

**`00166210` creates no `lua_State`.** It walks a priority-ordered intrusive list at `02CACC90`,
splices in a **statically allocated** `PUtilityScripting` at `01F2EAC8`, writes its vtable, and
tail-calls **`atexit`**. **Registration, not interpretation.**

The previous block had written: *"there is a live Lua interpreter in the process."* **Corrected in
place.** Entry 516 of the CRT initializer table proves an object is **constructed**; I let
*constructed* become *live interpreter* across one sentence. `DA-1` — whether a `lua_State` is ever
created — is unchanged.

### The system underneath it

The list head turned out to be shared: **64 references, 33 functions, and nothing in the database
cited it.** It is **Phyre's subsystem registry** — new document,
[`../22-Middleware-PhyreEngine/Subsystem-Map/the-fifteen-modules-registered-before-main.md`](../22-Middleware-PhyreEngine/Subsystem-Map/the-fifteen-modules-registered-before-main.md).

**Fifteen modules**, each a static object that links itself in before `main`: Serialization, Scene,
Animation, Rendering, PostProcessing, Geometry, Scripting, Physics, DynamicGeometry, LOD, Inputs,
Game, Hierarchy, DeveloperExtensions, Text. **Every name from its vtable's RTTI complete-object-
locator**, never from adjacency. Two `UtilityThreadPoolOrchestration` functions walk the chain.

### ⚠ And then the document was wrong, fifteen minutes old

`DB-3` — *"reading fifteen dwords settles it"* — so I read them rather than leaving a question I had
just described as cheap. **The dwords contradicted the document.**

Every readable module object holds, **in the image**: `vtable = 00D26BC0` (the **base** `PUtility`
vtable), `next = 0`, `key = 0xFF`.

1. **The "destructor" column was a guess presented as fact.** I had labelled the fifteen functions
   installing `00D26BC0` as destructors — the C++ idiom of downgrading to the base vtable on
   teardown. **But that value is what the object *starts* with**, which is what a **base
   constructor** leaves behind before the derived constructor overwrites it. **Neither reading is
   proven; the column is now headed "installs base vtable" and the evidence table carries the
   ctor-vs-dtor question at tier 8.**
2. **The "priority-ordered chain" was overstated.** The loop walks *while* `key < 0xFF`, and **every
   key is `0xFF`**, so `jae` fires on the first node and **each module splices at the head**. The
   ordering mechanism exists; the shipped data never exercises it. The chain is the reverse of
   initializer order.
3. **`PUtilityText` is in BSS** — the only one of the fifteen with no static image value at all,
   which also sharpens `DB-1`'s `0x38` oddity.

I also removed a third unsupported claim in the same sweep: slot 0 of every module vtable was
described as *"the shared unlink"*. **`005A0CB0` is unread.** It is now recorded as slot 0, role not
established.

> **Three claims, one block, all mine, all plausible, none checked before writing.** The pattern is
> not carelessness about evidence — each had *some* basis — it is **writing the interpretation in the
> same motion as the observation.** "Installs the base vtable" was observed; "destructor" was
> narration. "Walks while `< 0xFF`" was observed; "priority-ordered" was narration.
>
> **The cheap check that caught all three was reading the data the code manipulates.** Fifteen dwords.
> I had already written the document that described those dwords without ever looking at them.

### Not examined

The two walkers (`005A08E0`, 507 B; `005A0840`, 145 B) — **`DB-2`, and it decides whether this
registry is a startup manifest or a per-frame dispatch table.** `005A0CB0`. Whether anything lowers a
key at runtime (`DB-4`). `DA-1` and `DA-3` on the Lua side.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — `DB-2`: the registry is a lifecycle manifest, and one module can abort the boot

**Block: finishing `DB-2`, filed the previous block as the question that "decides how useful the list
is".** It did. Two function reads. `the-fifteen-modules-registered-before-main.md` gains its central
section; questions closed 369 → 370.

### The contract

**The subsystem registry is walked exactly twice per run — once to start the engine, once to stop it.
It is not a per-frame dispatch table.**

```asm
; 005A08E0  engine init
005A0A33  mov  rax, [rbx]             ; module vtable
005A0A39  call qword ptr [rax + 8]    ; slot +0x08 = per-module INIT
005A0A3E  jne  0x5a0ad5               ; non-zero -> ABORT THE WHOLE STARTUP
005A0A44  mov  rbx, [rbx + 8]

; 005A0840  engine shutdown
005A0866  call qword ptr [rax + 0x10] ; slot +0x10 = per-module SHUTDOWN
005A0869  mov  rbx, [rbx + 8]         ; return ignored
```

> **Any one of the fifteen modules can abort FFXII's startup by returning non-zero**, and the walk
> stops where it fails — every module after it in the chain never initialises. **Shutdown ignores
> returns entirely.** Failing to start is fatal; failing to stop is not.

Both are guarded by one flag at `02CACCA0`, so both are idempotent. Init allocates a `0xE0`-byte
object at `01F3F5B8` and **registers the shutdown with `atexit`** — `lea rcx, [rip-0x287]` resolving
to `005A0840`, then `call 00808B90`. **So the teardown walk is driven by the CRT at process exit, not
by game code.**

**The module lifecycle contract:** slot `+0x00` shared and unread, `+0x08` init, `+0x10` shutdown.

### A ledger tag declined

Both walkers carry `GFZ.V121.CodeDomain.Phyre.Runtime.UtilityThreadPoolOrchestration`. **Nothing in
either body read here is thread-pool work** — one allocates, walks and `atexit`s; the other walks,
tears down and frees. **The document records the tag as the ledger's claim and does not adopt it**,
per the standing rule that a GFZ annotation is a prior session's claim rather than a source.

### On the previous block's pattern

Last block's finding was that I had been *"writing the interpretation in the same motion as the
observation"* — three claims, all plausible, none checked. **This block was deliberately the
opposite: read both bodies first, and let the slot numbers name the roles.** The result named itself
— `+0x08` returning a value that gates the loop is an init; `+0x10` with the return discarded is a
shutdown — and no adjective in the write-up went beyond what the two loops show.

**The one thing that could still have been narration was the thread-pool tag, and it came pre-written
by the ledger.** Declining it was the same discipline applied to someone else's sentence.

### Not examined

**Slot `+0x00`** — `005A0CB0`, 100 B, the last unread piece of the lifecycle contract (`DB-5`).
Whether the three callers of the walkers (`00798603`, `007986D5`, `00798886`) are reachable from game
code. `DB-1` (`PUtilityText`'s BSS object and `0x38` offset) and `DB-4` unchanged.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — the input translator: the control mask is active-low, and `0x80` is proven neutral

**Block: `15-Input` — a genuine rebalance.** The previous several blocks had all landed in 22 or its
neighbours; Input is player-facing, had **no** work this session, and its largest unread function was
sitting behind two open questions. `binding-profiles-remapping-and-control-state.md` `audit.passes`
1 → 2. Questions closed 371 → 372, `AP-5` advanced.

`InputManager_TranslateBindingProfileToControlState` (`00197160`, 1,406 B) read — the whole per-frame
path from a binding profile to the bytes the game acts on.

### ⚠ The control mask is active-LOW

```c
if (active) mask[i >> 3] &= ~(1 << (i & 7));      /* CLEAR, never OR */
```

**A pressed control clears its bit.** A byte of `0xFF` means **nothing is held**. Sixteen digital
slots, one bit each, two bytes total. **Anyone reading that mask as "1 = pressed" inverts every
control in the game** — and the existing one-line summary called it "the inverted control mask"
without the mechanism, which is exactly the kind of phrase that is easy to read past.

The band tests appear as `(unsigned)(code-11) < 17`, `(code-28) < 125`, `(code-1) < 3`, confirming
`AP-1`'s corrected upper band of **28–152**. Codes 4–10 fall through every test — polled by nothing,
as the stack document already suspected.

### `AP-2` answered — with a constant, not the midpoint argument

The question had stood as *"it is the invalid-channel return and the midpoint of a byte; no comment
or constant confirms it."* **A constant does:**

```c
out = (v + 1.0) * 128.0 + ((float)currentByte - 128.0);   /* clamp [0, 255] */
```

`128.0` is a literal at **`008F3A2C`**, used **twice** — as the centre of the `−1 … +1` → `0 … 256`
map and as the reference the digital contribution is measured from. **`0x80` is the neutral.**

> **The midpoint argument would have reached the right answer for a reason that was not evidence.**
> `0x80` is the midpoint of a byte whether or not the engine agrees; what settles it is that the
> engine subtracts `128.0` from the current byte, so a byte already at 128 contributes nothing.

**And a nicety the read produced for free: the "invert" option un-inverts.** Axes 2 and 3 are
multiplied by `-1.0` (`01CEBC9C`) **unconditionally**, then by `-1.0` **again** when the per-axis flag
at `DAT_01F7FFE0 + 8` / `+ 9` is set. **The engine inverts those two axes by default and the setting
restores them.**

### Three binding modes

Mode **1** writes `0xFF` outright; mode **3** is a real stick axis; **mode 2 is how a keyboard drives
an analog axis** — two digital codes contributing **−0x7F** and **+0x80** to the byte's current value,
clamped to `[0, 255]`.

> **From the 128 neutral that gives 1 and 255, not 0 and 255** — asymmetric by one, because the
> contributions are −127 and +128 rather than ±127.5. **Recorded as the code computes it.** Rounding
> that to "0 to 255" would have been the same failure the last two blocks kept producing: writing the
> tidy interpretation instead of the observation.

### Not examined

**`AP-5` is advanced, not closed**, and the document says so: the *translation* is read, the **record
layout** is not — the group-1 entry stride has not been derived (`DC-1`). The device gate
`FUN_001C1990()[0] != 1 || FUN_00228480()`, which decides whether any binding is polled at all
(`DC-2`). And `008F3A28` = **120.0**, loaded on every iteration of the first loop and **used on no
path read here** (`DC-3`) — reported as a loose end rather than guessed at as a deadzone.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — `AP-5` closed, and two structures that are not the same structure

**Block: `15-Input`, second consecutive** — finishing the record layout that `DC-1` said blocked
`AP-5` from closing. Two questions closed, 373 → 375.

### The record

The two loops give it exactly. Loop 1 walks `int`s while `i + 1 < 0x10`; loop 2 starts at
`param_1 + 0x12` **ints**, advances `param_1 += 4` **ints**, and returns when `0xF < i + 1` —
**sixteen entries of four ints.** Because loop 2 reads `param_1[-2]` and `[-1]`, the pointer sits on
each entry's *third* field, so entry 0 begins at `+0x40`:

```
+0x00  int[16]    sixteen digital codes -> control-mask bits 0..15
+0x40  entry[16]  { mode, axisId, codeA, codeB }, 16 bytes each
= 0x140 bytes
```

### ⚠ And it is not the `.ini` profile — which is the finding, not a footnote

`keymap-ini-file-format-and-default-controls.md` derives a **`0x70`**-byte per-profile stride at
`0x01F80F90`, groups of **16 / 8 / 4**. **The translator's record is `0x140` with groups of 16 / 16.**

**Neither is wrong.** That same document records the translator being run **five times over a bank at
`0x01DF1480`** — a different address from `0x01F80F90`. **There are two structures: the authored
profile as stored from the `.ini`, and the runtime record the per-frame translator walks.**

> **The temptation was to reconcile them.** Two documents in one category, both about "binding
> profiles", both with a group-0 of sixteen — it reads as one structure described twice, and the
> tidy move is to assume one derivation is stale and quietly prefer the newer.
>
> **The addresses forbid it.** `01F80F90` and `01DF1480` are different banks, and the older document
> had already written down which one the translator consumes. **Reconciling would have produced a
> single confident layout that is wrong for both**, and a remapping tool built on it would edit the
> `.ini` copy and see nothing change.
>
> **The conversion between the two is unread and is now `DD-1`** — which is the honest shape of what
> is missing, rather than a merged table that hides it.

### Not examined

The writer that fills the `0x140` record from the `0x70` profile (`DD-1`), and the stride between the
five banked records. `DC-2` (the device gate) and `DC-3` (the unused `120.0`) unchanged from the
previous block.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — the control frame, and the same two facts proven from the other end

**Block: `15-Input`, third consecutive — the cap, and I should name the pattern rather than only obey
the limit.** Three blocks running I have ended a report with "next block rebalances" and then found a
reason to continue the same thread. **The reasons were each defensible and the aggregate is exactly
the drift the skill warns about.** This block is at the limit; the next one leaves the category
regardless of what it turns up.

`binding-profiles-remapping-and-control-state.md` extended again. `DD-1` sharpened, not closed.

### The frame

`InputManager_PollAndBuildControlState` (`00196FF0`, 356 B) is the **only** function in the image that
names the record bank at `0x01DF1480`. It builds an **18-byte** control-state frame on the stack —
`+0x00` a 16-bit digital mask, `+0x02` sixteen axis bytes — then runs the translator over the bank
five times.

### ⚠ Two prior findings re-proven from the producer

```asm
00197085  or      rax, -1
00197090  mov     word ptr [rbx], ax           ; mask = 0xFFFF
00197093  movabs  rax, 0x8080808080808080
0019709D  mov     dword ptr [rbx + 2], eax     ; axes 0-3 = 0x80
001970A2  mov     qword ptr [rbx + 6], rax     ; axes 4-11 = 0
001970A6  mov     dword ptr [rbx + 0xe], eax   ; axes 12-15 = 0
```

**The mask is initialised to all-ones and the translator only clears bits.** Active-low, now proven
from both ends: the producer sets every bit, the consumer only clears. **Two functions, one fact.**

**`0x80` is written into the axes before anything reads a binding.** `AP-2` was answered last block by
the literal `128.0` at `008F3A2C` inside the translator; **the initialiser answers it again, in a
different encoding, in a different function.** *A constant that shows up twice in two spellings is
worth more than a constant that shows up once* — and neither reading needed the "midpoint of a byte"
argument the question had been stuck on.

**An asymmetry that came free:** only axes **0–3** get the neutral. Axes **4–15 are zeroed**. Centred
quantities and uncentred ones, in one structure — consistent with two sticks plus twelve slots that
are digital-derived, though **which of the twelve any shipped profile actually writes is not
established.**

### `DD-1` — direction settled, writer still missing

`0x01DF1480` has **one reference in the entire image** and it is a read. The `.ini` profile at
`01F80F90` has 8 references in 6 functions. **No function references both.**

> **So the writer does not name the bank's address** — it reaches it through a pointer. That is a
> sharper question than the one filed last block, and it keeps `DD-1` honestly open: **until the
> writer is found, it is not established that editing the authored profile reaches the game.**
>
> **The tempting shortcut was to call the single `lea` "the bridge" and close it.** It is a `lea` into
> a read path; calling it a write would have been the same failure as reading `mov [obj], vtable` as a
> destructor two blocks ago — **naming a role from a position rather than from what the instruction
> does.**

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — Movement: the avoidance RNG nothing owns, and the order opcode space

**Block: `06-Movement-And-Navigation` — the rebalance I committed to last block, taken regardless of
what Input still had open.** Player-facing, 485 functions across 4 documents, untouched this session
apart from one falsification pass. `battle-locomotion-steering-and-recovery.md` `audit.passes` 2 → 3.
Questions closed 376 → 378.

### ⚠ `AM-6` — the detour draw is the CRT `rand`, and that settles a determinism question

All three randomised recovery functions call exactly one external import, the same one:
**IAT slot `008C3098`**, which the **import table** resolves to
`api-ms-win-crt-utility-l1-1-0.dll!rand`. `ResetAvoidanceStateRandomized` (278 B) **calls nothing
else at all**.

**Not the shared gameplay LCG at `00328370`. Not either Mersenne Twister.** The stream
`rng-streams.md` classifies as *external, external mixed authority* — 48 sites across 30 functions,
shared with anything else in the process that draws.

> **So a blocked unit's detour cannot be made reproducible by synchronising the game's own RNG**,
> because the draw is not the game's own. Two machines running the same battle diverge on the first
> obstruction.
>
> **This is engine evidence for a rule the project already holds on design grounds** — foe avoidance
> is a *local decision*, decided independently on each machine, with no counterpart to send. The rule
> was written from the multiplayer side; the binary agrees with it from the other.

*The slot was resolved from the import directory rather than by citing the RNG document's table —
the same claim, re-derived, because a slot number copied between documents is exactly the sort of
thing that goes stale.*

### `AM-5` advanced — the order format, and 53 opcodes that are really four behaviours

`DecodeOrderGoal` (`00312730`) reads `order+0x00` as a **u16 opcode** and `order+0x02` as an **s16
parameter**. The opcode space is `0x4000`-based, resolved through three range tests and an MSVC
two-level jump table (indices `00312B10`, image-relative targets `00312AF8`):

- **`4000`–`401F` — thirty-two opcodes, one shared case**, parameterised `opcode − 0x3FFF`
- **`4026`–`4035` — sixteen opcodes, one shared handler** `002367A0`, parameterised `opcode − 0x4026`
- four specials: `4020`, `4022`, `4023`, `4024`
- **two holes: `4021` and `4025`** — inside the band, routed to the same target as an unknown opcode
- a separate compare chain from `0x4124`

> **Forty-eight of the fifty-three low opcodes are two behaviours with an index.** The order language
> is much narrower than its opcode count suggests, and `DE-1` records the consequence: **two handler
> reads would explain 48 of them.**

**`0x4022` is now placed.** This document's own open item describes the fixed-speed escape testing
`record[5] == 0` and "a literal `0x4022`". **It is an order opcode with its own decode case at
`0031289E`** — not a magic number, and that item is annotated in place rather than left implying one.

**Not one case body is read**, and `AM-5` is marked advanced rather than closed for exactly that
reason: the format and the dispatch are established, **the semantics of all six cases are not**.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — two randomised movement systems, two RNGs, opposite determinism

**Block: `06-Movement-And-Navigation`, second consecutive — `DE-1`, filed last block as "the cheapest
large return left in this category".** It was: **two function reads explained 48 of the 53 order
opcodes.** `battle-locomotion-steering-and-recovery.md` extended again. Questions closed 378 → 379.

### The sixteen, and the thirty-two

**`0x4026`–`0x4035`** share `002367A0` — **`BattleUnit_FindByDefinitionSelector`**, already named in
the ledger, called with `opcode − 0x4026`. **Sixteen opcodes are sixteen ways of naming a unit: the
opcode's number *is* the definition selector.** The order carries no handle.

**`0x4000`–`0x401F`** reach `00312DF0`, whose core is a bounded rejection sampler:

```
offset = (LCG() % (2 * param + 1)) - param       /* uniform in [-param, +param] */
```

retried **up to nine times** (`cmp eax, 8; jg`), with the actor's radius — the `maxss` of
`ActorRuntime_GetClass3FloatPair` — written through an optional out-pointer. **The order language's
largest family is "go somewhere random near here, within ±N", and N is the order's own parameter.**

### ⚠ The finding that matters: it is a *different* RNG from last block's

`00312DF0` calls **`00328370`, the shared gameplay LCG**. The avoidance recovery documented last
block calls **`rand`** through IAT `008C3098`.

| system | stream | reproducible |
|---|---|---|
| order-goal random point, `4000`–`401F` | shared gameplay LCG | **yes, if the LCG is synchronised** |
| avoidance / detour recovery | process-global CRT `rand` | **no, ever** |

> **Two randomised movement systems inside one subsystem, on two streams, with opposite determinism
> properties.** An authored order that says *wander within ±N* can be reproduced on another machine;
> a unit deciding for itself how to get round an obstacle cannot.
>
> **Neither fact is visible from the other.** Had this block not been run, `AM-6`'s "movement
> randomness is unownable" would have stood as the subsystem's story — and it is only half of it.
> **The generalisation "movement is non-deterministic" would have been wrong for the larger half.**
>
> And this is a real `call`, not one of the seven inlined LCG copies the RNG document records — **so a
> hook on `00328370` sees this draw**, which the earlier inlining finding says is true of only one
> draw in eight.

### On the block choice

`DE-1` was filed with an explicit prediction — *"two reads would explain 48 of the 53 opcodes"* — and
that is exactly what it cost. **Writing the estimate into the question made the next block's decision
trivial and checkable**, and the estimate held rather than being generous to itself.

### Not examined

**What the index `opcode − 0x3FFF` selects** — thirty-two opcodes share the sampler and differ only by
that index (`DE-4`). The rest of `00312DF0`'s 237 bytes, the four special opcodes' cases, and the
`0x4124` band (`DE-3`).

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — 351 functions that are one template

**Block: `24-Compiler-And-CRT-Runtime`, the tool's own pick (debt 515) and a genuine rebalance** —
the previous two blocks were Movement, the two before that Input. New document:
[`../24-Compiler-And-CRT-Runtime/Generated-Adapters/the-return-value-adapter-family.md`](../24-Compiler-And-CRT-Runtime/Generated-Adapters/the-return-value-adapter-family.md).
Documents 208 → 209.

### What was learned

The category's composition line lists **`Runtime.Callback` 402** with no document behind it. The
census gives **409 functions, 13,254 bytes — 32 bytes average**, of which **351 carry
`ReturnValueAdapter`**.

**All 351 are exactly 26 bytes.** Not approximately: the size column has a single distinct value
across the whole family. Four were disassembled and compared instruction for instruction — **they
differ in one field, the `call` target**:

```asm
push rbx / sub rsp,0x20 / mov rcx,rdx / mov rbx,r8 / call <target> / mov [rbx],rax / ... / ret
```

**The adapter converts a returning function into a writing one**, so a caller that knows only
*(context, argument, result\*)* can invoke anything taking one argument and returning a value.

Each appears **exactly three times** in `.rdata`, and walking back from each slot to the RTTI
complete-object-locator names the three:
`PClassDescriptorForType<T>`, `PClassDescriptorAbstract<T>`, `PClassDescriptorConcrete<T>` — **the
same descriptor triple this database already records for `PClusterHeaderBase`**. Sampling 120 gives
**93 distinct classes**, one or two adapters each, 11 unresolved.

### Why it is worth a document rather than a footnote

> **A function count that includes these is inflated by 409, and by 351 for one template.** This
> database reports coverage as *functions cited of 34,270*; **351 of that denominator are one
> compiler idiom repeated per class.** That does not make the measure wrong, but it does mean the
> denominator is not a measure of distinct behaviour, and nothing had said so.
>
> It also sits beside `the-thunk-layer-and-why-call-counts-disagree.md`, which exists for the same
> reason from the other direction. **Two documents now, on the two ways this binary's function count
> misleads.**

### Method

The whole block used the four-instrument reachability discipline written down three blocks ago:
the size column and the tag census to find the family, disassembly to establish the shape, **a raw
data-section address search** to find where they are installed — which a code-body scan cannot see —
and the RTTI walk to name the owner. **No step inferred a role from a position**; the vtable identity
came from the complete-object-locator, not from what the neighbouring slots looked like.

### Not examined

The 46 `OwnedBufferResetAdapter` functions (`DF-1`). **Which reflected operation the adapter actually
serves** — the shape is established, the semantics are not, because no descriptor vtable's slot
layout has been enumerated (`DF-2`). The full 351-adapter class map, so "one or two per class"
remains a sample statement (`DF-3`). And nine one-off dispatchers whose tags name real mechanisms.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

---

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — `DF-2`: slot 5, and a document that corroborates instead of discovering

**Block: `24-Compiler-And-CRT-Runtime`, second consecutive** — finishing `DF-2` on the adapter family
documented last block, using the method that question named: enumerate a descriptor vtable's slots.
Questions closed 379 → 380.

### The adapters are descriptor virtual slot 5

Forty adapters, each of their three `.rdata` appearances walked back to its vtable start and the
offset computed: **slot 5, 120 appearances out of 120.** Not a majority — every one.

The descriptor kinds those appearances belong to are **ForType 40, Abstract 40, Concrete 37**, plus
**2 `ConcreteWithoutDefaultConstructor`** and **1 `Header`** variant — so the triple is not always
literally *ForType/Abstract/Concrete*, and a few classes substitute a variant into the third position.
**Recorded because the sample showed it, not smoothed into "always three".**

### What slot 5 is

The call targets are ~301-byte functions tagged **`Phyre.ScriptVM.ObjectPointerAccessor`**, and
`005BEC00` opens by calling **`00682980`** — which the Lua-bindings document identifies as the
`"PhyreObject"` registry-key lookup that gates every script-supplied userdata.

> **Slot 5 is "take a script value, check it really is a Phyre object of this class, and hand back the
> pointer".** The 26-byte adapter exists only to give that a uniform `(context, value, result*)`
> shape.

### ⚠ And this corroborates an existing document rather than discovering anything

`phyre-script-object-accessors.md` **already maps 747 accessors over 412 distinct bound types**, and
its table lists `005BEC00` → `PAnimation::PTimeScaleOffsetController *`. **The RTTI walk here reached
`PTimeScaleOffsetController` for that adapter's descriptor triple independently, from the vtable
side.** Two routes, one class.

> **I checked that document before writing the claim, not after** — the failure mode this session
> recorded twice (`CR-1`, where I filed a question the database had already answered; and the reach
> map, where I nearly re-derived a published census). **The check cost one grep and changed the
> framing of the whole section from "found" to "confirmed".**
>
> The adapter *layer* is genuinely new — that document does not mention it, and grep for "adapter",
> "351" and "26 B" in it returns nothing. **What is not new is the accessor family underneath, and the
> document now says so in its own words.**

### ⚠ A number that does not reconcile, reported rather than reconciled

**351 adapters against 747 accessors.** Fewer than half the accessors have one. The obvious
explanation is the pointer/reference split — but that is **402 pointer accessors against 351
adapters**, close and **not equal**, so it is not that.

**Filed as `DF-4` rather than rounded into "roughly the pointer accessors".** The unread
46-strong `OwnedBufferResetAdapter` family may account for part of the gap.

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

## 2026-09-08 — `xiionline-61` — why an area's music changes as the story advances

**Block: `18-Audio` — the rebalance I committed to, and the one substantial category with no work
this session at all** (2,064 functions, 13 documents). `how-the-music-changes-when-you-change-area.md`
`audit.passes` 0 → 1; its longest-standing open item closed.

### The table

**`003EAA70` (229 B, explicitly unread) is a paged, run-length-compressed field → track map with a
per-run threshold ladder.**

Base `0x02B5E0D0`; `[+4]` is the highest field id covered; pages are `fieldId / 128`. A run is

```
u16 packed  -> start = packed & 0x7FF, len = packed >> 11
u8  count      u8 default      entry[count] = { u16 threshold, u8 trackId }
next = run + 4 + 3*count
```

**An 11-bit start and a 5-bit length in one `u16`** — a run spans at most 32 field ids, and the
encoding bounds field ids at 2,047, which comfortably holds the 1,154 the navimap table counts.

The ladder walks while `progress > threshold`, taking each entry as it passes and keeping the last;
otherwise the run's default. **Track `0` is converted to `-1` by a `cmove` — silence and "unmapped"
are the same answer to the caller.**

### ⚠ The join is the finding, not the ladder

The ladder key is `FieldData_GetVariable0` (`00265270`) — and
[`../14-Story-Event-Quest-Hunt/persistent-flag-banks.md`](../14-Story-Event-Quest-Hunt/persistent-flag-banks.md)
**already documents that function as the story progress variable, and already documents the same
threshold-ladder idiom** applied to it.

> **Two subsystems — area music and the persistent flag banks — are driven by one number through one
> shape, and neither document knew about the other.** The mechanism was published; what was missing
> was that the music table uses it.

### A discrepancy that wasn't

The flag-banks document writes `FieldData_GetVariable0` (`00265270`, **22 callers**) and the ledger
gives the function 28 bytes. **I read that as a size disagreement and went to the binary**: the body
ends at `0026528B`, so it is `0x1C` = **28 bytes**, and the document says *callers*, not bytes.
**Nothing to correct** — and its published C reproduces the disassembly line for line.

*Recorded because the check cost one disassembly and the alternative was either a false correction or
an unexamined doubt.*

### Not examined

Where the table comes from — nothing read writes `0x02B5E0D0`, so **whether a modder edits the
executable or an archive file is unknown** (`DG-1`). What a track id indexes (`DG-2`). And the one
that changes the modding advice: **whether any shipped run has more than one ladder entry, or whether
area music is in practice constant per field** (`DG-3`).

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.
### ⚠ The correction: the base address was wrong, and the scanner found it

Last block published the field → track table at **`0x02BDE0D0`**. It is **`0x02B5E0D0`** — two digits
transposed while computing the RIP-relative target by hand from `mov r8, qword ptr [rip + 0x277364c]`.
Recomputed from the bytes: `0x003EAA84 + 0x0277364C = 0x02B5E0D0`.

> **Nothing in re-reading the document would have caught it.** The wrong address is well-formed, in
> the right section, and reads plausibly. **What caught it was a cross-reference scan returning zero
> references while its control passed** — and a table the mapper demonstrably loads cannot have zero
> references. **The contradiction was the signal, and taking the instrument's word over my own
> arithmetic is what resolved it.**
>
> This is the inverse of the session's recurring failure. The usual error has been trusting my own
> reading over an instrument; here the instrument said *nothing is here* and the right response was to
> doubt the address, not the scan. **A scan with a passing control that returns zero for something
> that must exist is a statement about the query, not the binary.**

Corrected in the document, the register, and both index files — 8 occurrences.

### `DG-1` answered: the map is loaded, not linked

`0x02B5E0D0` sits **past `.data`'s raw content** (1,631,232 raw against 18,511,152 virtual) — BSS. The
correct-address scan gives **4 references in 3 functions**, one of which writes it:

```asm
003EAB64  mov  edx, 0x20                  ; element size
003EAB69  lea  r8,  [003EAB90]            ; associated handler
003EAB70  lea  ecx, [rdx - 0x17]          ; = 9, the resource selector
003EAB73  call 0x51ED10                   ; FFXII.ResourceDataLoading
003EAB78  mov  qword ptr [02B5E0D0], rax
```

**The field → track map is resource 9, element size 0x20 — it is not in the executable.** A music mod
edits a data file.

**And that requalifies `DG-3`.** Whether any shipped run uses more than one ladder entry **cannot be
answered from the image at all**, because the runs are in the resource. The question is not merely
open; **it was open against the wrong artefact**, and now says so.

### Not examined

Which archive entry selector 9 resolves to — `FUN_0051ED10` (153 B) is unread, and reading it would
name the file (`DG-4`). What triggers the load: `003EAB60` is not a static initialiser, has no direct
call sites, and its address is in no data table (`DG-5`).

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

`003EAB60` installs the music table with `[02B5E0D0] = FUN_0051ED10(9, 0x20, &FUN_003EAB90)`.
`0051ED10` (153 B) opens by calling `0032E400(9, 0x20)`, which is **a size lookup**:

```c
table = ((void **)0x022C51F0)[selector];        /* selector < 0xFF */
if (table && index < 0x400) return table[index];   /* a dword, returned as-is */
size = FUN_0046DE70();                          /* the default */
if (size > 0) size = (size + 0x3F) & ~0x3F;     /* rounded only on this path */
```

The result goes to `0036A190` (10 B, `Runtime.MemoryAllocatorSupport`) for a pointer, then to
`0032E620` (340 B) with selector, index, pointer and size.

**So `9` and `0x20` name a size, not a file.** `0x02B5E0D0` holds an **allocated buffer**.

### ⚠ The retraction

Last block I wrote: *"the field → track map is fetched as resource `9`, element size `0x20`, through
the resource-loading path"* and *"a modder edits a data file, not the binary."*

**The BSS half stands. The "fetched as resource 9" half is withdrawn.**

> **`0051ED10` carries the ledger tag `FFXII.ResourceDataLoading`, and I turned that into a
> mechanism.** "Resource data loading" is a fair name for a function that sizes and allocates a
> resource buffer — **the tag was not wrong; my sentence was.** I read a category label as if it were
> a description of behaviour, which is precisely what the standing rule about GFZ annotations exists
> to prevent, and I did it while quoting the tag as my evidence.
>
> **The function that would have settled it, `0032E620` (340 B), was one hop away and I did not
> open it.** Stopping at a plausible tag is cheaper than stopping at an unread function, and it reads
> the same in the write-up.

`DG-4` is reframed rather than answered: the premise "which archive entry does selector 9 resolve to"
was wrong. The live question is **what populates the buffer**, and whether it touches a file at all.

### What came out of it

**`DG-6`, a general facility nobody has looked at**: a table of size tables at `0x022C51F0`, indexed
by a selector below `0xFF`, each up to `0x400` dwords. The rounding asymmetry is a small tell —
**table entries are returned unrounded and only the default is aligned to 64**, which says the tables
hold already-aligned sizes.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

### What the fourth function does

```c
g_currentSelector = selector;        /* 0x022C51D8 */
g_currentIndex    = index;           /* 0x022C51DC */
if (index < 0) return -1;
if (!FUN_003EB0D0()) goto 0x0032E742;
/* a 0x28-byte descriptor is copied in from the caller's stack struct */
```

`FUN_003EB0D0` (41 B) is a **presence check** — one byte at `0x02B5E111`, in a `0x38`-stride loop
**bounded to exactly one iteration**. A walk written for an array and compiled for a single entry.

The addresses cluster into one state block: the table pointer at `0x02B5E0D0`, another pointer at
`+0x18`, the flag byte at `+0x41`; and the selector/index globals sit **`0x18` before** the
size-table array at `0x022C51F0`. Filed as `DG-7`.

### ⚠ The point of the block: what I did not conclude

**Four functions in — `0051ED10`, `0032E400`, `0032E620`, `003EB0D0` — nothing touches a file.**
The chain sizes, allocates, records state, and gates.

> **The obvious move after retracting "it comes from a file" is to assert "it does not".** Both are
> unsupported. What is established is narrower and duller: *the file has not appeared in four
> functions*, and the remainder of `0032E620` plus its alternate path are unread.
>
> **A retraction creates pressure toward the opposite claim**, because the reversal feels like the
> finding and reads as decisive. It is the same error in the other direction — and it would have been
> harder to catch, because "no file" is consistent with everything read so far.

`DG-4` therefore stays open with its evidence updated rather than being closed either way.

### Every RIP target computed with the tool, not by hand

Last block's error was a transposed digit in a hand-computed RIP-relative address. **Every address in
this block — `0x022C51D8`, `0x022C51DC`, `0x02B5E111`, `0x02B5E0E8` — was resolved by script from the
instruction bytes.** The one habit change that would have prevented the previous error, applied.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.
this category had no work this session) **and a REOPENED question, which outranks a fresh one.**
`gambit-evaluation-and-enmity.md` `audit.passes` 2 → 3. Questions closed 382 → 383.

### The answer

**`DAT_01DFE0B8` is the frame-rate scalar, not the 2×/4× game-speed scalar.**

Triple `{01DFE0B4 int, 01DFE0B8 f32, 01DFE0BC f32}`, image values `{1, 1.0, 1.0}`, written together
as `{2, 2.0, 0.5}` by `001EDDC0`.

**`01DFE0B4` has 212 references in 121 functions**, and the dominant idiom is
`imul <reg>, [01DFE0B4], N` with **N = 30, 60, 90, 150, 240, 900, 1200, 1500, 9000, 30000** — every
one a duration in 30 fps frames: half a second through five hundred.

> **The question had been argued from the writer for two rounds.** The first answer was NO on a false
> premise about restoring a default; the reopening was on the writer's `2.0f` against a shipped
> `1.0f`. **Neither side looked at what reads the integer**, and that is where the answer was —
> 212 references saying the same thing.
>
> **Direction of effect closes it.** A 2× game-speed selector makes the same durations elapse
> *faster*. This **multiplies every authored timeout**: a 30-frame wait becomes 60, the same
> wall-clock time at twice the frame rate. **Speeding up and doubling timeouts are opposite
> operations, and the engine does the second.**

### And the writer was only reachable through a `jmp`

`001EDDC0` has **no direct callers and no data pointers**. It is reached by a single **`E9` tail call
at `0022BE7D`** from `0022BDF0` (146 B, `FFXII.CoreRuntimeScheduling`), which passes the mode in
`ecx`.

**A call-only reachability scan reports this function as unreached** — which would have made the
whole triple look like dead configuration. The four-instrument habit (`call`, `jmp`, code operand,
data pointer) is what kept it alive.

### Not examined

**What decides the mode** — `0022BDF0`'s argument is unread, and it determines whether every authored
timeout in the shipped game runs doubled (`DH-1`). **Whether any path writes a mode other than 2**:
none was found, but the writer set was not exhaustively enumerated, so *"the engine only knows 1× and
2×"* is unproven rather than established (`DH-2`).

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — green.

---

## `FrameLimit` — `DH-1` and `DH-2` answered, and the question that should not have been asked — 2026-09-08

**Document:** `19-Save-Load-Settings/Game-Settings-Config/game-settings-ini-and-runtime-config.md`
§12–§15 (audit pass 5). **Corrections applied to:**
`09-Combat-AI-And-Gambits/gambit-evaluation-and-enmity.md`,
`00-Engine-Core-Runtime/what-the-engine-fixes-at-boot.md`.

### What a player gets from this

**The 30/60 option is not a frame cap.** FFXII's durations are authored as PlayStation-2 frame counts
at 30 fps; choosing 60 makes the engine multiply every one of them by two so that a fifteen-second
timer stays fifteen seconds. The ten `imul <reg>, [01DFE0B4], N` constants in the image — 30, 60, 90,
150, 240, 900, 1200, 1500, 9000, 30000 — are **every one an exact multiple of 30**, i.e. 1, 2, 3, 5,
8, 30, 40, 50, 300 and 1000 seconds. That is what fixes `N` as a 30 fps frame count.

**And at 30 the game asks Windows for a high-resolution timer; at 60 it does not.**

### The chain

`GameSetting.ini` `FrameLimit` → `config[18]` at `0x01F82D20 + 0x48` → `0017D8F0` (11 B) →
`0022BDF0` (`ebp = FrameLimit == 1`) → `E9` tail call → `001EDDC0`.

**The two branches are mutually exclusive**, and the 30 branch **writes nothing**: `{1, 1.0f, 1.0f}`
is the `.data` initialiser, not a fallback, and it *is* the 30 fps configuration. That is why the
30 fps path was invisible to every scan for writers.

Also established: `01F82E38` is a **60-Hz gate with one write and thirteen `cmp …, 0` readers**;
`FUN_0033F020` has **one caller and seven targets** (six `call`, one `jmp`) that run only at 60; the
config object is constructed from `00808D78` (`MSVCCRT.ExecutableStartup`) **before `main`**, which is
why the getter needs no null check; and `FFXII_TZA.exe` accepts **`-nologo`**.

### `DH-2` closed from the source rather than from a failed search

The old formulation could only say "no 4× variant was found" — an absence from an unbounded search.
The value-name row for `FrameLimit` is **exactly two entries**, and `0022BDF0` reduces even those to a
boolean, so **any value that is not exactly `1` yields `0`**. A hand-edited ini cannot reach a third
mode. **`01DFE0B4` is `1` or `2`.**

⚠ **The 4× the question was reaching for is real, in a different system:** `g_speedMode` at
`0x01FD4A98`, multiplier table `0x00908BA8` = `{1.0f, 2.0f, 4.0f}` — the player's fast-forward.
**Speed multiplier and time base are opposite operations** and cannot interact, because `01DFE0B4`
has exactly one writer.

### ⚠ The instrument failure this block caught in itself

`01F82E38`, `01F82E3C`, `01F82E40` and `01F82D20` all sit in `.data` **past `SizeOfRawData`**
(`0x18E400`). A naive file read of those addresses returns **`.pdata` bytes** — the first read of
`01F82E38` came back `0x0005AC43`, which is not a value, it is the next section. **They are BSS and
load as zero.** The triple at `01DFE0B4` *is* inside raw data and genuinely ships `{1, 1.0f, 1.0f}`.

> **New rule: before quoting an "image default", check the address against `SizeOfRawData`, not just
> against the section's virtual range.** A virtual-range check maps BSS to whatever follows it on
> disk and returns confident garbage.

### ⚠ And the process failure, which is the more important one

After the work was done, `check_already_answered.py` found that
`15-Input/Binding-Profiles/keymap-ini-file-format-and-default-controls.md` **already carried**
`GameSetting_GetFrameLimit → *(u32*)(GameSetting + 0x48)` and
`Startup_ParseCommandLineAndSelectFrameBase → HostTiming_Select(FrameLimit == 1)` in its address
table — while `DH-1` was being filed elsewhere as "**unread**".

**`check_already_answered.py` must run before a question is filed, not only before one is assigned.**
This is the second instance of that rule this week (`CR-1` was the first) and the first at the scale
of a whole thread rather than a single question. **What §12 adds over that row is everything past the
name**; the input document named the road and did not say where it went — which is exactly why a
cross-category index sweep is cheaper than re-deriving.

### Corrections

| where | was | is |
|---|---|---|
| gambit doc §8f | `[01F82E35]`, `[01F82E30]` | **`01F82E3C`**, **`01F82E40`** — hand-computed RIP targets, recomputed with the tool |
| gambit doc | `FUN_0033F020` "six sub-initialisers" | **seven** — six `call` and one `jmp` tail call |
| boot doc, falsification row | "`0022BDF0` … is the writer of that global. The ordering is the guard." | it writes it **only at 60**; at 30 nothing writes it and the `.data` initialiser `1` is what makes `2 / DAT_01DFE0B4` safe |

**Two of the three corrections are the same error class already on this log** — a RIP target computed
by hand, and a call census that missed a tail jump.

### Not examined

`DH-3` the thirteen `01F82E38` readers · `DH-4` the seven 60-Hz-only initialisers · `DH-5` what
`-nologo`'s global gates · `DH-6` what a shipped `GameSetting.ini` actually contains.

### Independent re-derivation, unplanned

Reaching §12 from the timing side required re-deriving the settings schema from scratch — the 31-count
loop, the key table, the 31×9 value-name table, the 16-byte preset matrix, `ApplyQualityPreset`, and
the 31-byte mask. **Every one matched §1–§11 exactly.** Two independent derivations, no disagreement.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions` — see the run below.

---

## The post-process chain, named from the engine's own shader constants — 2026-09-08

**New document:** `22-Middleware-PhyreEngine/Post-Process-Chain/the-post-process-passes-and-their-shader-constants.md`
(311 lines, audit pass 0). **Category rebalanced** from `19-Save-Load-Settings` to
`22-Middleware-PhyreEngine`, which `coverage_balance.py` named (6,768 functions, 13 documents,
debt 483).

### What a player gets from this

**FFXII TZA ships two entirely different ambient-occlusion implementations**, not two settings of
one — HBAO at `00190760` and a scalable/Alchemy-style pass at `00191E90`, with **adjacent, disjoint**
parameter blocks on the same context object.

**And both split every occlusion parameter between characters and scenery.** `radiusChr`/`radiusSce`,
`biasChr`/`biasSce`, `intensityChr`/`intensitySce`, `g_RChr`/`g_RSce`, `g_AngleBiasChr`/`g_AngleBiasSce`,
`g_ContrastChr`/`g_ContrastSce` — six pairs, with only `fadeoutScale` shared. Character contact
shadows in FFXII are tuned independently of the world's, and **a modder raising AO strength must
change both halves of every pair** or the two drift apart.

**The outline around menu text is a full-screen post-process pass** (`00190260`,
`FontBorderSetting`/`FontBorderColor`), not a property of the font renderer — so it costs the same
for one word as for a full menu.

### How the passes were identified — without guessing

All 65 functions tagged `Phyre.Rendering.NativeQualified` were disassembled and every RIP-relative
`lea` target decoded. **17 reference a string; 12 of those reference shader-parameter names**, and
the set of names a function passes *is* its identity: `SMAA_THRESHOLD` and `searchTex` can only be
SMAA, `g_HBAOResolution` and `g_NumDir` can only be HBAO.

> **This is a naming instrument that costs one pass over a tag group and needs no call-graph
> reasoning at all.** It works because the engine looks up shader constants by string.

**Five of the 17 are not passes** and were inspected individually rather than assumed: one carries an
`EASTL vector` diagnostic, and four point at three bytes that merely decode as text. **The count in
the first draft said "twelve of 65 reference names; the rest reference none" and was corrected before
publication** — 17 reference strings, 12 reference parameter names, 48 reference nothing.

### The mechanism, complete

```c
p = FindShaderParameter(shader, "SMAA_THRESHOLD");   /* 0018D240 */
if (p) {
    offset = *(u16 *)(p + 0x10);
    size   = *(u16 *)(p + 0x12) & 0x1FFF;
    if (offset + size <= *(u32 *)block && offset >= 4)
        memcpy((u8 *)block + offset, &value, size);  /* 00861EBA */
}
```

**The constant block is `{ u32 declaredSize; u8 data[]; }` and offsets are measured from the block
start**, which is exactly why `offset >= 4` is enforced — an offset of 0–3 would overwrite the size
field the bounds check just used. Read at four independent sites, identical at all four.

**A missing parameter is silently skipped.** Adding a constant to a replacement shader achieves
nothing unless its *name* is one the pass already hard-codes.

### SMAA is stock, and that is a checkable claim

| `context+0x184` | THRESHOLD | MAX_SEARCH_STEPS | ..._DIAG | CORNER_ROUNDING |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 0.15 | 4 | 0 | 0 |
| 2 | 0.10 | 8 | 0 | 0 |
| 3 | 0.10 | 16 | 8 | 25 |
| 4 | 0.05 | 32 | 16 | 25 |

**Those are the published SMAA reference presets exactly** — LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/ULTRA. FFXII wired the
stock header to a quality integer and tuned nothing. **One departure:** upstream's LOW sets
`CORNER_ROUNDING` to 100 (saturating the corner pass off); this build uses 0. The comparison against
the upstream header is recorded as **tier 3, external** — the four ladders themselves are tier 1,
read from the branch chains and stored immediates.

`SMAA_RT_METRICS = float4(1/w, 1/h, w, h)`, computed at `001928EC`–`00192917`.

### Real constants recovered

`3.141592502593994f` / `180.0f` (degree→radian, twice) · `500.0f` (`projScale`, a literal, **not**
resolution-derived) · `600.0f` (the scalable-AO bias divisor) · `-2.0f` (`projInfo`) ·
`0.05f`/`0.10f`/`0.15f` (SMAA thresholds).

### What was deliberately not claimed

- **Which setting selects which AO** (`EA-1`). `HDAO`'s three values and the scalable pass's
  `widthAO`/`heightAO` are suggestive; the selector was not read.
- **That `0018FE00` is FXAA** (`EA-7`). It is the only remaining candidate for the `PostAA` key's
  other value, and it writes no FXAA-specific constant. **A candidate is not an identification.**
- **The context object's class** (`EA-2`) — the reflection schema is the route, not taken here.

### Not examined

`EA-3` the descriptor size word's top 3 bits · `EA-4` what fills the AO blocks · `EA-5` the 48
string-free functions · `EA-6` `001945B0`.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions`, `verify_doc_addresses` — see the run below.

---

## The Lua loader — `DA-3` answered, and the engine will compile source — 2026-09-08

**New document:** `23-Third-Party-Libraries/Lua-Core/the-lua-loader-and-what-it-accepts.md`
(333 lines, audit pass 0). **Category** `23-Third-Party-Libraries` (1,000 functions, 4 documents,
debt 200) — reached by census rather than by the balance tool, which named `24`; the Lua
parser/compiler surfaced there only because `ParserCompiler` matched a `Compiler` filter, and its
honest owner is the third-party category.

### What a modder gets from this

**You can write plain Lua text. You do not need to compile it.** The engine's loader passes
**`mode = NULL`** to `luaL_loadbufferx`, and both of Lua 5.2's `checkmode` guards are
`if (mode && …)`, so **neither guard can fire** — bytecode and source are equally accepted, and the
only thing that chooses between them is the chunk's first byte.

**And the Lua build is not stock.** Read out of the header writer's own immediates:

```
1B 4C 75 61 | 52 00 01 04 | 08 04 04 00 | 19 93 0D 0A 1A 0A
"\x1bLua"     5.2, fmt 0,    size_t 8,     LUAC_TAIL
              LE, int 4      Instr 4,
                             lua_Number 4  <-- FLOAT, not double
```

**`lua_Number` is 4 bytes.** Stock Lua 5.2 on x64 uses `double`. Two consequences, both actionable:
anyone producing bytecode must build `luac` from a Lua 5.2 configured with a 4-byte `lua_Number` or
the header check rejects it; and **every number in every FFXII script is single precision** — integer
arithmetic is exact only to 2²⁴ = 16,777,216.

The header size is confirmed twice: the writer emits 18 bytes, and the checker at `0082E490` compares
**`0x11` = 17** — `LUAC_HEADERSIZE − 1`, the signature byte having already been consumed.

### The chain

```
00684D10 / 00684F60 / 00686280   ->  00683C30 (mode = NULL)  ->  00812F70 luaL_loadbufferx
  ->  00810180 lua_load  ->  0081DF50 luaD_protectedparser  ->[lea]->  0081D790 f_parser
       first byte 0x1B ? 0082E910 luaU_undump : 0082BE30 luaY_parser
```

A pleasing detail in `f_parser`: the compiler derives `'b'` from the byte it just compared against —
`lea edx,[rsi+0x47]` with `rsi` proven `0x1B`, and `0x1B + 0x47 = 0x62`. The `'t'` on the other arm is
a plain immediate. `008C2A10` resolves through the import directory to `VCRUNTIME140.dll!strchr`.

### Reachability — and a control error caught in the act

`0081D790` has **zero callers**; it is address-taken once, at `0081DF7C`. All four instruments were
run, **each with a control positive for that instrument**:

| instrument | control | control result | `f_parser` |
|---|---|---|---|
| `E8 call rel32` | `008216A0` `luaV_execute` | 3 hits | 0 |
| `E9 jmp rel32` | `008216A0` | 1 hit | 0 |
| code memory-operand | `0081D790` itself | 1 hit | **1** |
| raw data-section qword | `005A2B80` | 3 hits | 0 |

> ⚠ **The first attempt used `0082E910` (`luaU_undump`) as the control for the qword scan and it
> returned zero — correctly**, because `luaU_undump` is reached by a direct `call` and is never stored
> as a pointer. **A control must be positive for the instrument being tested, not merely "known
> reachable".** A reachable-by-other-means control makes a working scan look broken and would have
> made a broken one look working. Caught before it reached a conclusion.

### `DA-3` answered — and its own premise disproved

`DA-3` asked what supplies a chunk, and said *"identifying the loader would convert `DA-1` from a
runtime question into a static one."*

**The loader is `00683C30`. The hope is wrong.** Its callers `00684D10` and `00686280` are two of the
very executors `DA-1` reports as *reached only by virtual dispatch*. **Load and execute are the same
functions** — never two unknowns to be attacked from opposite ends, but one. **`DA-1` stays a runtime
question**, and a breakpoint on `00684D10` remains the way to settle it.

What `DA-3` does settle is the more useful half: **the capability**. *If* a chunk is supplied, it may
be source text, and that is now static and certain.

### Also recorded

`00683C30` **swallows compile errors** — it stringifies the message and immediately pops it, returning
`false`; a null data pointer returns `false` without touching Lua at all. Two inner calls are written
as `FUN_` in the document rather than named, because `lua_tolstring`/`lua_settop` is **inferred from
the argument constants (`-1`, `-2`) and neither function was disassembled**.

### Not examined

`EB-1` what the three callers pass · `EB-2` whether `luaL_loadfilex` is engine-reachable (**the other
half of `R-4`**) · `EB-3` `00814B30`, a `lua_load` caller with no callers · `EB-4` whether any caller
reports a compile failure · `EB-5` the parser's eight functions, 4,654 bytes, identified by their
error strings and unread.

### Gates

`check_links`, `check_consistency`, `check_questions`, `verify_doc_addresses` — see the run below.

## 2026-09-08 — `Database/Tools/` enters version control

**94 files, 1,036,319 bytes, all text, never staged.** 32 tools, 20 evidence dumps, 42 handoff
documents and seals. Not gitignored by any rule — simply never added. [the research-provenance index](RESEARCH-PROVENANCE.md).

**14 of the 15 files a document names are tools**, and the citations are load-bearing: the line
at the top of this page, the mission prompt's *“update `GfzEnv.ps1` after every certified
checkpoint”*, and the audit log's use of `GfzEnv.ps1` as the authority on which parent is
certified. **A clone could not run any of them, nor check the lineage claim against the file
that makes it.**

> **The seals and handoffs are tracked as history, not as truth.** They are prior sessions'
> claims about the binary, and the order is unchanged: binary > fresh derivation > prior
> annotation. Tracking a claim makes it checkable; it does not make it right.

## 2026-09-08 — measured against the charter's own standard for the first time

[the reconstruction-grade audit](RECONSTRUCTION-GRADE-AUDIT.md). Charter §38's oldest first action, closed.

**The headline is not a percentage, it is which questions can be asked.** Of §3's seven
dimensions, **`Behaviour` has no record type at all** — preconditions, postconditions,
invariants, state read and written, side effects, branches, state transitions, failure paths,
edge conditions, and nowhere in the schema to put any of them outside three contracts. No
keyword proxy was invented for it, because that is how `DR-1` happened.

> **And this ledger's own instruments have been understating the database.** Documents name
> **5,319–5,812 of the 7,376 function entries they cite (72.1–78.8 %)**; `functions.tsv`
> carries a name for **677 (9.2 %)**. §3's *Identity* is very nearly satisfied over the cited
> population and no tool here could see it, because **a name written in a document had never
> flowed back into the export.**
>
> **Fixed the same day.** `knowledge_db.py` now carries a `doc_names` table (6,448 rows) and
> three queries over it; `DU-1` is closed and the range is a range on purpose — see
> [the schema page](KNOWLEDGE-SCHEMA.md) §7. **The export back to the ledger is not done and
> must not be automatic** (`DU-2`): a name in prose is a claim, and charter §28 forbids bulk
> speculative labelling.

**One subsystem answers 20 of §27's 21 completion questions** — the `GameSetting.ini`
configuration object, scored by hand — **and fails §26's readiness gate on a single item:
runtime validation.** The category around it does not follow: the save image layout, version
stamps, locale and file locations have no contract. **One contracted subsystem is not a
contracted category.**

## 2026-09-08 — `SteeringRecord`: the fifth GAME type, and the first found by chasing a pointer

[the two steering slots](../06-Movement-And-Navigation/Facing-And-Steering/the-two-steering-slots-and-what-a-character-resumes.md) — acting on
[the reconstruction-grade audit](RECONSTRUCTION-GRADE-AUDIT.md) §2, which named six documents
standing at `BEHAVIORALLY UNDERSTOOD` over categories with **no `_types/` entry and no
contract**. Two of the six are `06-Movement-And-Navigation`. That category now has a type.

**GAME types 4 → 5; fields on a GAME type 109 → 141.** `SteeringRecord`, **`0x80` bytes**, 32
fields — **13 named, 19 `UNRESOLVED`**, 95 of 128 bytes accounted for (**74.2 %**), and the five
gaps are reported rather than rounded away because the size is declared.

> **A record no previous instrument could see.** It is reached only as
> `actor → +0xB8 → +0xF0`, so a displacement scan cannot find it (`[rax+0x28]` says nothing
> about `rax`) and a global cross-reference scan cannot either (nothing references it). The new
> `record_field_scan.py` follows the chain register by register inside each body — **55
> functions, 32 fields, two hand-derived controls reproducing.**

**And it is not allocated.** `shl rax, 7` at `00269F9F` proves the size and the shape at once:
**two inline records** at context `+0x120` and `+0x1A0`, with `+0xF0` a cursor selected by one
byte. Every character carries two complete steering states and a depth-one push/pop pair flips
between them.

## 2026-09-08 — goal 2: the fifth reconstruction, and the first stub-free differential test

`Reconstruction/Game/Story/flag_banks.{h,c}`, tested by `diff_flag_banks.py`:
**2,754 comparisons, 0 mismatches** over 4 operations × 153 ids (**67 negative**) × 3 payloads.

synthetic payload. `INTEGRATION` and `STATE-TRANSITION` need the running game.

## 2026-09-08 — the sixth contract, over script dispatch

[the script-to-native bridge contract](../01-Script-VM-Native-Bridge/CONTRACT-script-to-native-bridge.md). **Interface 5 → 6 of 228 documents; GAME types
6 → 8**; store 185 → 187 types, 763 → 770 fields, 0 errors.

Charter §29 lists **script dispatch first** among high-leverage knowledge, and
`01-Script-VM-Native-Bridge` owed **both** remaining rungs. Both are now paid, and the
sub-runtime rung-debt list is down to **one document**.

> **`VmValueStack` tiles its `0x284` exactly and reports no gaps** — four parallel 64-entry
> arrays whose bounds agree with each other and with the push guard `cmp eax, 0x3F`. **63
> slots are usable, not 64**: a push writes only when `depth < 0x3F`, so slot 63 exists in
> every array and nothing can put a value in it.

**The finding that matters for goal 2:** the subsystem has **two tag spaces offset by one**.
The value stack tags `1 = int32, 2 = float32`; the result entry tags `2 = int32, 3 = float32`.
**A `2` means different things in the two records**, and one shared enum silently returns a
float where an int was meant.

**Still L1 for the category.** §13 is empty, and this contract covers the *convention* over
seven verbs — not the 1,224-row population that obeys it.

## 2026-09-08 — the seventh contract, and sub-runtime rung debt reaches zero

[the group-completion contract](../05-Spawn-Encounter-Lifecycle/CONTRACT-group-completion-and-respawn.md). **Interface 6 → 7 of 229 documents; GAME types
8 → 10**; store 187 → 189 types, 770 → 776 fields, 0 errors.

> **The last document owing a rung below `RUNTIME VALIDATED` is cleared.** Every labelled
> document now owes exactly one rung and it is the same one for all of them: runtime
> validation, blocked on `EXP-001`. **No undone desk work remains on that list.**
>
> **And the list was never the corpus.** 15 of 229 documents carry a readiness label at all
> (`DJ-1`), so this says the labelled documents are consistent with the ladder — not that the
> database is.

**What was learned about the game.** Killing a group is a **checklist, not a counter**:
`(memberMask & deadMask) == memberMask` over a 64-entry ledger of 64-bit masks, so order does
not matter and overkill does not count. **A respawn wait is the same wall-clock length at 30
and 60 fps** — the elapsed reader divides the frame counter by `(int)(scale × 30.0f)`, which is
the one place in the subsystem where the frame rate is deliberately cancelled out.

**And the three respawn fields are script-only.** Four instruments, controls positive: each
accessor has exactly one caller and it is a VM verb wrapper. **Nothing native relates the
cooldown to the elapsed seconds**, so `EE-1`'s unit question is a script-side convention and a
static instrument cannot settle it.

**Still L1.** §13 is empty, and **584 of the spawn record's 592 bytes have no known reader.**

**63 claims: 44 `PROVEN`, 11 `UNKNOWN`, 2 `VERY HIGH`, 6 with no confidence column** — the last

**Five of the eleven unknowns are the same claim**: *“any runtime observation — none exists”*,

## 2026-09-08 — goal 2: a differential test answers an open question

`Reconstruction/Game/Math/angle_wrap.{h,c}`, tested by [`diff_angle_wrap.py`](../../../Reconstruction/Tests/Differential/diff_angle_wrap.py):
**8,280 comparisons against the shipped bytes, 0 mismatches**, over 4,140 distinct float bit
patterns. **Reconstructions 5 → 6, harnesses 5 → 6. Parity-certified stays 0.**

> **The test's third job was not parity at all.** After checking both reconstructions against
> the image, it ran **the two shipped forms against each other** — and answered `DY-1`:
> **3,668 of 4,130 inputs disagree, 88.8 %.** The `fmod` form is not the identity on its own
> output range (`1.0` → `1.0000002`, `+pi` → `-pi`, `-0.0` → `+0.0`); the loop form leaves an
> in-range value exactly alone. **A differential harness can measure the game, not only a
> reimplementation of it.**

**And the loop form cannot terminate for `|x| >= 2**27`** (about `1.34e8`), where adding `2pi`
is absorbed by float rounding. **6 of 4,140 inputs are in that state, verified by
`x + 2pi == x` rather than assumed**; 4 more were too slow to emulate and are reported as
skipped with their iteration estimate, not folded into either total.

## 2026-09-08 — the head of `DT-1`, and a central update gate found under it

[one word decides which fifteen subsystems tick](../00-Engine-Core-Runtime/Per-Frame-Update-Gate/one-word-decides-which-fifteen-subsystems-tick.md).

`0028BB50` (2,387 B) **was the largest function in this database that no document had ever
cited**. Reading it turned up something more useful two frames up: **`01E0C464` is a single
16-bit word that gates fifteen groups of per-frame updates** — sixteen `test` sites, all
against the same global, never a computed index. **A clear bit stops a whole group outright**,
with no degraded mode. Six of the fifteen groups are `UI`-owned.

> **Nothing in the database had cited `01E0C464`, `0028E290` or `002507C0`.** A 2,387-byte
> function nobody had looked at was sitting two calls below a central update loop, which is
> the argument for `DT-1` in one sentence.

**The residual itself is deliberately unnamed.** Degrees-to-radians by `pi/180` *and*
`pi/180000`, `±pi/2` clamps, `atan2f`, the `FrameLimit` time base, and a list of `>= 0xD78`-byte
nodes walked under a `UI` bit. **It is not `Phyre::PCameraPerspective`**: that object is `0xBB8`
(`BE-6`), so the size refuses the guess the arithmetic invites. `DZ-1`.

**`DT-1`: 112 → 111 uncited residuals.**

## 2026-09-08 — the enable word has no writer, and that is the finding

[one word decides which fifteen subsystems tick](../00-Engine-Core-Runtime/Per-Frame-Update-Gate/one-word-decides-which-fifteen-subsystems-tick.md) §10, reopened.

**`01E0C464` has 16 references in the entire image. All 16 are in one function and all 16 are
`test`.** Operand instrument over every function body, control positive. **A word that fifteen
per-frame update groups depend on, and nothing addresses it by name to change it.**

> **The route is one address away.** `001AF470` (3,179 B) takes the address of its neighbours
> `01E0C460` and `01E0C470` and stores each into a consecutive 8-byte slot of an object — **a
> registry of pointers to globals**. A write through such a table is invisible to every
> address-keyed scan, which explains 16 reads and no writes and names where to look next.

**Not claimed:** that this registry is the documented
[282-command console](../00-Engine-Core-Runtime/Developer-Tuning-Console/the-282-command-parameter-console.md)'s
binding. That console's block is a different object and nothing here connects them.

**And RTTI cannot name the node.** None of the three functions in the chain contains an
indirect call or a `[reg + 0]` qword load, so the node is never dispatched through and there is
no vtable to follow — **a bounded negative**, the same shape as `BE-6`'s eight vtable-less
singletons. Naming it needs the allocator or a string.

## 2026-09-08 — the gate suite read end to end, and classified into three kinds

Charter §38's *automated integrity checks* has been `[~]` throughout. **Every gate has now been
run and its output read.** The useful result is that they are not all the same kind of thing:

| gate | kind | state |
|---|---|---|
| `check_links` | correctness | **green** — 11,4xx relative links resolve |
| `check_consistency` | correctness | **green** — no address described two ways |
| `check_questions` | correctness | **green** — table and checklists agree |
| `check_denominators` | correctness | **green** — 17 → 0, 2026-09-08 |
| `check_doc_claims` | correctness | **green** — 2 → 0, 2026-09-08 |
| `check_layout_units` | correctness | **green** — 1 → 0, and it was a false positive |
| `verify_doc_addresses` | correctness | **green** on everything edited |
| `check_self_contradiction` | judgement | **3, all dispositioned** — question-ledger rows quoting an absence |
| `check_absence_claims` | judgement | 28 candidates, read line by line, no unhedged false claim |
| `check_named_not_cited` | **backlog** | a work queue, not a defect list |
| `check_located_not_read` | **backlog** | **52 functions**, of which **14 are vendor or compiler output** |

> **Conflating the three kinds is what left findings unread.** A *correctness* gate should be
> green and its hits are defects. A *judgement* gate's hits are questions, and twice this week
> the answer was **the document is right and the checker is wrong**. A *backlog* gate measures a
> frontier and will never be green — treating its output as failure is precisely why nobody
> read it.

**This block: 2 → 0 on `check_doc_claims`, 1 → 0 on `check_layout_units`.** One was a real
mislabelled cross-reference; the other was a false positive fixed **in the gate**, whose
self-test still fires on the deliberately broken block afterwards.

`00565E00` (1,914 B) was the second-largest function no document had ever cited. **Four
instruments, controls positive for each — `E8` 0, `E9` 0, code memory-operand 1, data qword 0**
— and its only reference in the image is a single `lea` inside its 38-byte registrar. **A
